# Continuity State

## Canon status

The world, power rules, protagonist, relationships, terminology, antagonist ladder, and ending constraints are established in `bible/` and `outline/series.md`. Those are canon.

`outline/volume-01.md` and `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md` are **approved and now drafted**. Chapters 1–10 exist in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0001/`. Where this file and a drafted chapter disagree, the chapter governs the prose and this file is corrected.

**Volume 01, Chapters 1–49 are written. THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE.** Chapters 11–20 are in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0002/`, Chapters 21–30 in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0003/`, Chapters 31–40 in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/`, and **Chapters 41–49 in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/`.** Batch 0003 was reviewed and repaired in place; its fourteen findings, the month-boundary rule they produced, and one structural date defect that was found and deliberately left open are all in § *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0003 on review* and the section that follows it. Batch 0004 was reviewed and repaired in place; its three blocking findings, six non-blocking findings and the length note are in `reviews/batch-0004.findings.md` and in § *The Batch 0004 review pass*. **Batch 0005 drafted against the corrected thirty-day month and honored it: days 41–52 are dated by their ordinal of the month after October or by their plain season day number, and no document in the batch carries a month name.** Its day clock, the day-to-chapter map actually used, its numbers, its locks, the closed-volume facts and its proposed canon changes are in the sections below under *Batch 0005*. **Batch 0005 was reviewed by hand after the pipeline's standing failure to load the `novel-reviewer` subagent, and every finding was applied in place; see `reviews/batch-0005.findings.md` and § *The Batch 0005 review pass*.** **The volume as a whole was then closed: the volume-wide mechanical audit, the quotation-mark balance, the day clock, the figures, the duplication scan, the Ch 29 / Ch 30 overlap and the spelling decision are all in § *The Volume 01 close pass*, and the close-up is `reviews/volume-01.findings.md`.** **That close was then audited a second time and six findings were applied, five of them repairs the first pass had recorded as done and one of them a required section the first pass had not written at all; the second audit, the fifteen chapters it touched and the quotation-mark decision are in §8 of the close pass.**

## Opening state (as established by Chapters 1–10)

Marek Vale is twenty-nine, an agricultural engineer and irrigation surveyor at the Lantern Reach Work Cooperative in Tarrow Fen. The third consecutive White Harvest has failed. The charter expired at midnight on the eighteenth day and the annual inspection fell on the same day. After Chapter 20 he has no contractor, no charter, a wrecked coupling, a hand-built holding seal that is standing and wet at one corner, a month of compost he cannot buy, a branch at Weir Row that bleeds and is due to be cut again on the twenty-seventh, seven days, an instrument he no longer may open, and a sister on a cart.

Marek is **still Stage 0** at the end of Batch 0002, with two contacts behind him — one supervised (Chapter 6) and one deliberate and unsupervised with no return (Chapter 9) — neither of which he can repeat, and **no third**. Sera Quill refuses him the pit in Chapter 11 and gives the reason. The Chapter 20 reading is **Sera's, taken by a licensed reader with the copper clip at the seam, and Marek is on the boards outside the housing and does not touch the instrument.** There is no rootmark. Stage 1 is not sustained until the end of Volume 01.

## Rules locked for later prose

- Rootlight is metabolic energy in living tissue; it cannot create soil, water, seed, food, or obedience.
- Every rootwork operation needs a living anchor, a compatible path, and a return.
- Human living anchors are possible only through recorded consent, a stop signal, and a safe disconnect. Shared anchors keep separate channels and never fuse minds.
- Rootwork is a licensed profession; a living bind is the forbidden arrangement, not the craft itself.
- False Season changes local root, soil, heat, and migration conditions; it cannot conjure sky weather.
- Bridge capacity is named by load: local conduit, seed-route bridge, regional bridge. A practitioner does not own a bridge's consequences.
- Public proof requires an independent external read and at least two corroborating records. A surrendered memory alone is not enough.
- A caretaker handoff is staged. Replacement seedhearts must be planted, load-tested, and carrying flow before the old link is isolated.
- The recovered living control seed can open local valves but cannot command the running Engine without a caretaker link and compatible return path.
- The first White Mercy phase is a limited core test in Volume 13; the final pruning window opens the decisive activation in Volume 16.

Four governs and Ch 9 is fixed to match | Ch 1, Ch 3, Ch 9 |

| Season month | **The season month is not to be named, and the *records* never name one.** **Corrected twice on review, and the earlier version of this row was simply false.** The Batch 0003 pass first removed an unsupported *November* from this row, then claimed that no month is named anywhere in Chapters 1–30; the prose does not support that second claim either. What is actually true: **October is named four times in Chs 24, 28 and 29, in speech only, and it is the name of the previous month** — the flock went up the fen road on the tenth of October, which is season day 10. **No document, entry, license sheet, use-log line, notice or letter in Chapters 1–30 carries a month name.** *February* (Chs 5, 11, 12, 14, 15) and *January* (Ch 12) appear only as foreseeable months ahead, and *April* once in Ch 9 as something a character remembers. **The governing anchor for the season is Ch 1's forward reference to Michaelmas, which places it in autumn.** A later writer **may keep saying October in speech** — Lissa Vale and Tova Reed both do, and the compound is load-bearing — and **may not attach a month to a document, may not name the month days 21–30 fall in, and may not contradict October as the name of the month days 1–20 fall in.** See § *Batch 0003 day clock*. | Ch 1, Ch 9, Chs 21, 24, 28, 29, 30 |

The return the certificate calls for falls on day 31 | Ch 21 |
| The weep at the six o'clock corner, on the twenty-first | **a quarter of an inch, measured twice with two rules, pump on the idle, no change since the twentieth. Not a leak at the load stated.** Still a quarter inch at the end of the batch | Ch 21, Ch 30 |
| Gael Senn's first examination entry | **nothing found.** Never *all well* | Ch 21 |

| Compost put into the return main, on the twenty-first | **one hundredweight**, off the wall, at four shillings, weighed at the gate. Against a specified **four hundred hundredweight a month**. Shortfall **three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight a month = £79 16**, specification **£80** | Ch 21, Ch 24 |
| The seed house's bin | fed for eleven years by the pasture flock, twice weekly from mid-September; **empty of anything but sweepings, annex ash and two loads of spent bark since the middle of October**; the bed has lost a degree every nine or ten days since and stands at **four degrees** | Ch 24 |
| The new run | **thirty-one yards**, cut off the return main at a point **twenty-two yards up from the eleventh rod** (i.e. above the fence post, which was left alone), along the north ditch of the outer field, let into the ditch-head scour. fiber bed the whole length, four willow sets driven and lashed with green withy. **A local conduit** in the trade's naming, and nothing above that | Ch 21, Ch 22 |
| The outer field | **twenty-two acres.** **Nineteen** come up after the run; **three at the top end do not** | Ch 23 |
| The three acres | **Marek's own ground**, on his own account since the third season back, because the cooperative would not have them; top of the field, thirty inches of gravel over flints; **four hundred and ten yards of ditch dug by hand across three autumns**; lost in a day and a half from Ch 29 onward, banks slumped, ditch become a crack, will not crop until the third season after this one | Ch 21, Ch 23, Ch 29, Ch 30 |
| The pump at full draw | **the head holds 1,100 where it would not hold 900 before the run.** Entered as a dial reading, not as a day of water. The frame hums from about 1,040 | Ch 23 |

The highest ever on the board is **261**, in the spring of the year Aldren went, so 254 is **seven short of the best figure on it**. Teale has read the board thirty-one years, put his own hand on the chalk each time, and sent each figure up the fen road **before the district** | Ch 22, Ch 23, Ch 24 |
| Standing green fodder | **nineteen acres** fenced for sheep, with no sheep, no cart, and a winter coming; **1,712 tons** standing, Tova Reed's figure | Ch 23 |

| The pail timing | ninety counts on the sixteenth; three minutes on the twenty-second; four and a half on the twenty-fourth; **five minutes forty and five minutes fifty** on the twenty-sixth, **four timings, three of them by Gael Senn because she does not trust a number one man has timed** | Ch 26 |
| The Weir Row branch | **the same branch that surfaces in the bottom of the low quarter**, nine miles down the fen. It had nothing coming up it since the spring before last; it was cut on the night of the tenth, and the twenty-seventh cut it a third time | Ch 26, Ch 27 |
| The third face | cut **to specification at the bottom of the opened ground**, i.e. below the three feet Marek opened with the crowbar and the shears, **square to the run of the branch, where Marek's to-scale drawing of the ninth said it would land.** Stamped **N-2214/K**, third time in a decade. A hold against the item was **released on Monday**; nobody may see the job sheet | Ch 27 |
| The bleed from the opened seam | continuous and entered as *unchanged* four times over **seventeen days** (the seam was opened on the night of the tenth, so the count to the twenty-seventh is seventeen, and the first pass's *eleven* was a slip corrected on review), then **not reduced — it stopped**, before the second hour of the twenty-seventh. **cause not established** | Ch 27 |
| The schedule of damage | **thirty-nine houses at £1 10 for fourteen days, two public pumps at £1, total sixty pounds ten shillings**, under the district water-supply bylaw sixth part, against *the body last responsible for the supply to the ground, being the cooperative of Lantern Reach, whose charter on the node expired at midnight on the eighteenth.* Payable in fourteen days; representations in fourteen days; **no appeal and no power of appeal**. Served in person on eleven houses on the morning of the twenty-eighth | Ch 28 |
| The cooperative's box | **£4 5**, being £5 11 less the £1 14 Marek put in himself. The £12 6 levy is gone into Sera's fee. First stated in the prose in Ch 24 | Ch 24, Ch 28, Ch 29, Ch 30 |
| The cooperative's entry on the *cause* line of the form | **not established**, with a paragraph underneath saying that the cause lies below the ground supplied, that the cooperative has no standing on that ground, and that the document which would establish it cannot be obtained by this cooperative. Written at the counter in front of Perrin Osse, who entered the hour and said nothing | Ch 28 |
| The cooperative's own statement, entered on the thirtieth | **eleven hundred words**, in Marek's own name as engineer of record, Sera Quill signed under hers, Gael Senn signed third, Anneke Roos read it aloud first, Lissa Vale signed the witness column: that the cooperative's whole account of the cause of the damage **is a rumor with a witness in it**, that the one licensed reading on the node is worth to the district and not to them, and that a thing that is entered can be denied but not lost. Goes out with the form inside fourteen days | Ch 30 |
| The Assembly roll for Tarrow district | **two names: Sera Quill, and Idris Vane**, 61, eleven miles from the Fennmere counter, no paid work since his wife died, a better reader than Sera and available. **Blocked by the fourteen-day notice, not by money.** Not a licensed reader who is available this month | Ch 30 |
| The second pathhound | **an old black-and-white collie with the left ear off**, standing in the dead bed at the bottom of the low quarter on the twenty-sixth, and in nine inches of moving water in the Weir Row channel on the afternoon of the twenty-seventh. **The same animal in both places, nine miles apart, because the channel came alive.** Belongs to a dead woman's grandson, **Joss Pyle**, who brings it milk. Not the weir animal | Ch 26, Ch 27 |
| The three refusals of the bypass, and the whole refusal | **the twenty-seventh, sixth hour**: S. Quill (a second license this month on an unfed node doubles the shortfall under one signature), A. Roos (no contractor without a charter, no money, and a Crown-format repair would be a second Crown-shaped document), G. Senn (*I would do it, and wanting it is the objection*), J. Byre (he will not go down four feet), and Marek's own fifth reason. **A third section-nine margin note**, signed by nobody but Marek, ending *this is worth nothing against a cut* | Ch 27 |
| Tova Reed's second refusal | **a draw of 380 turns/day for four days (1,520 turns) on the three acres**, taken out of the shallow flow under the north annex. **Three days is nothing; the fourth morning takes about a foot of head off a bed at four degrees with an empty bin and the March's tin in the press.** Refused under the seed-vault condition, ninth page, never withdrawn. Also refused, by **Sera Quill**, as a return substitution: a body is a return, not a source | Ch 29 |
| Sera Quill's fourth refusal | a rule she made for herself nineteen years ago in a different office, that a book does not get to be a reason. **She does not name what it is about and does not give it a number, and it is a different pair of names from the other three** | Ch 30 |
| Marek's arm, fourth check | **nothing**, on the eighth, the twelfth, the twentieth and the thirtieth. She does not say the *twice a week* thing a fourth time | Ch 30 |
| People who read the open book between the fourth and the thirtieth | **none.** Not the district, not a neighbor, not one of the sixteen households. Anneke Roos's entry | Ch 30 |

**Mill wheel and water turns are the same measure.** The Fennmere mill wheel is turned by the cooperative's return race, so wheel turns measure the water coming back. This is why the tally board corroborates the loading test, and it is the record the volume climax needs. Stated once, in Chapter 2, and it must not be contradicted.

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 31–40

A later writer must not change these without recording the change. Several are load-bearing for the volume climax in Chapters 41–49. **Nothing in this batch changed a figure established earlier; every new figure is additive and is listed here.**

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| Season day 31 | **the first of a calendar month**, a Tuesday, and the first of the month after October. **Gael Senn's second monthly examination falls on it and is not moved** — and this one *is* the return the certificate calls for. Day 40, the last day of this batch, is the tenth day of that month | Ch 31, Ch 40 |
| The weep at the six o'clock corner, on the first of the month | **a quarter of an inch, measured twice with two rules, pump on the idle, no change since the thirtieth of last month. Not a leak at the load stated, and the load stated has not been applied: the load test was the twentieth day's act, on a certificate, there is no charter to hang a second one on, and Ms Quill has said she will not spend another act on that head in that condition.** Still untouched at the end of the batch. Do not resolve before Ch 41 | Ch 31 |
| The beat, seventh logging | eight counts in ten minutes with one run of five, in a book with **no licensed person present**, and the taker says so out loud. **cause not established.** Nobody names it | Ch 31 |
| The return line on the first of the month | **nothing put into the return main. None available to put. Specification four hundred hundredweight a month. Last put in: one hundredweight, on the twenty-first of last month. To the body: four pounds five shillings. Witnessed: none. Licensed person present: none.** Gael Senn's entry, signed by her, and the wording distinction is now policy: *nothing found* is what you find at the wound; the return line records a thing that did not happen | Ch 31 |
| The mill wheel, Rowan Teale's board, Movement 4 | **181 on Tuesday, the first of the month** — and it is *not a flow*: no water in the race at the second hour, the sluice below the mill dry to the sill, the wheel turned by the miller's own arm for a count of ninety. **And on Wednesday, the ninth of the month, no figure at all: the sluice is shut, the race is out of water below the second sluice, and there is nothing to chalk.** Both entered by Dunmar Fitch as a thing reported. The four Movement 3 figures (126, 131, 254, 231) are unchanged | Ch 31, Ch 40 |
| The Assembly circular | **Rootwright Assembly, Tarrow district, circular, second issue of this season**, one sheet, **form A/4**, dated by hand **the twenty-ninth**, four clauses, signed **H. Pike, President**, with an embossed stamp and the line *issued under the standing delegated authority of the Assembly*. Clause one restates the three requirements. **Clause two: where a graft has been performed under a named engineer's license and the return is not maintained, the question of the license is a question for the Assembly and not a question for the engineer alone.** Clause three invites reports of unlicensed grafting **to the district office, and clause four says reports are not to be sent to the Assembly directly.** Four hundred went out to every address in a district water record; Lantern Reach is on the list **because of the report of the twenty-fourth**, and Perrin Osse did not add it | Ch 32 |
| The cooperative's reply to the circular | **one page, unsigned, on seed-house paper, from the seed house**, carrying the three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight, the fact that the license sheet is a single page in the cooperative's own book and has never been filed, and the month, day and hour a licensed engineer put the shortfall in her own hand. It cannot be a reply in the Assembly's sense and is sent anyway, on the thirtieth's reasoning | Ch 32 |
| The cooperative's address | **none.** A box at the Fennmere post was paid out of the charter and the charter lapsed at midnight on the eighteenth | Ch 32 |
| The Crown's controlled burn | **the south embankment windbreak, nine hundred yards, between the two old fence stakes at the ends of the willow and elder**, firebreaks cut to the mill race, a crew of nine, a licensed man in attendance, at the district's cost, **to be executed between the sixth and the twelfth day of this month on a day of the district's choosing**, entered on the node's record as an executed item. Emergency-maintenance schedule, hazard measure, **form EM/2** | Ch 33 |
| The consent | **a page and a half that says, six times in different words, that the body signing it is satisfied the root line beneath the windbreak is not a line the body relies on for the supply of the node, that its severance is not a damage to the node, and that the body will not thereafter be entitled to say the district's entry was made without its consent or against its interest.** The cooperative holds no charter, so the only thing it can give is a signature, and the district knows it | Ch 33 |
| The seed ground on the sheltered side of the windbreak | **about forty feet of low growth at two hundred and sixty yards: milfoil, sweet gale, and a mat of fen sorrel.** Four years to come back. Tova Reed's figure for the March road is nine thousand people | Ch 33 |
| The windbreak, the cooperative's own cutting | **one hundred and six feet of green withy cut by Ilsa Kern on the Sunday of last month for four sets of willow, entered in the working notes in Marek's hand with the hour, initialled by Anneke Roos as an opinion, and read aloud at a counter in Fennmere by the district.** Ilsa had said *it will not be a windbreak there in a year*; in this batch she is the one who says **we did this first** | Ch 33, Ch 40 |
| The notice to Idris Vane | **one page in Lissa Vale's seven-column form, dictated by Marek at the seed house table at the sixth hour on the fourth day of this month, in the presence of L. Vale, D. Fitch, A. Roos and G. Senn, and delivered in person at Vane's own house at the eleventh hour the same day. Notice runs fourteen days from the hour of delivery. Witnessed in duplicate. It ends Out. Back** | Ch 34 |
| Idris Vane's four conditions | **fourteen days' notice, in person; not to be told what the first reader read, and the first reader's record sealed in an envelope under Lissa Vale's name, not to be opened before he has read the node and not at all if it is broken early; his figures unedited, in his own hand and under his own name, to the district and to the cooperative, both or neither; and his price** | Ch 34 |
| Idris Vane's price | **fourteen pounds ten**, not moved in four years, for a reading, a finding and an attested copy; **it is one day and he does not do it in one day.** Against a bill of sixty pounds ten and a box of four pounds five. **The notice is served; the money is not, and the notice therefore moves the earliest possible independent reading to about the eighteenth of this month, which is after the hearing's form and probably after the water** | Ch 34 |
| The form, altered at a stranger's table | **the *amount disputed* box struck out, nothing written in it, and *the cooperative does not dispute the amount* written at the top of the page in Marek Vale's own hand and not in a clerk's, on Idris Vane's advice. The cause is still disputed. If the cooperative loses the cause it owes the whole of sixty pounds ten** | Ch 34 |

It holds the far end of the long line.** Its people for the willow's roots are **the fence's feet**; the line itself is **the long line**, and it runs **under about two hundred yards of the south embankment windbreak and comes up at the toe in about nine places**, then north-west to the weir and on into ground that is theirs. **A long line cannot be replanted** | Ch 35 |

No fee. The copy goes into the file and cannot be recalled by anybody who is not an officer of the office** | Ch 37 |

The district does not produce the works schedule and that is unchanged and structural | Ch 37 |

He asked Perrin Osse for the date of the sitting and was not told it. The box is closed and should now be treated as spent; the next writer must not spend it a second time** | Ch 37 |

He says he would rather do it in front of the officer because an act the officer has seen is an act** | Ch 38 |

Back. Back: the same day, the eighth hour of the day. Fifty minutes, in the district's hands, with no licensed person present. The district holds the receipt** | Ch 38 |

She has broken the cooperative's own inside-cover rule in the first line of its only clean book and says a clerk of a cooperative lost a charter on the seventeenth of a month over one hour on one entry** | Ch 38 |

| The March's tin | **still in the press under the ledger, not moved, **twenty days** at risk, the tin having gone into the press on the nineteenth of last month, and the annex does not reduce it by one day, with Lissa Vale's own undertaking under her signature: the seed house does not undertake to move it and the trust does not ask it to** | Ch 39 |

The answer: a measure is varied by the district, by a new form, on a date nobody has. Anneke Roos required Marek to say in one sentence whether he objects; he said he has no standing to object with. It is entered and not objected to** | Ch 40 |

## Records and instruments now in play

**SUPERSEDED IN PART AT THE END OF VOLUME 01.** This list is as it stood at the end of Batch 0004. The instrument is no longer in the press under the ledger; from the twenty-second of the month after October it is on the middle of the seed-house table. The standing pressure log and the monthly report are now charter conditions. The use log has been read aloud in public twice. The final position of every one of these is in § *Volume 01 closed — the facts, in one place* and § *New locks from the drafted Batch 0005*, and those govern.

- **The cooperative's open book** (Ch 4, from day 6): the log, the acceptance column, the work book, the loading test, and Marek's own error, on the seed house table, open to any member, stranger, or sub-office clerk without notice.
- **The standing pressure log and the monthly root-change report** (voted Ch 10, item two): the cooperative's own obligation. The Crown adopts them as charter conditions in Ch 47. Do not invent them earlier.
- **The use log** (Ch 10): Lissa's book, first line the tenth. Form is *Date. Instrument. Person holding it. Purpose. Licensed person present. Out. Back.* It governs the fieldbook for the rest of the volume and is read aloud at the climax.
- **Sera's raw notes** (Ch 6): nine pages, two days of quarter-hourly figures, in Marek's hand, including the four strokes past the refusal on day 3. She holds the originals; he keeps nothing.
- **Marek's weir page** (Ch 7): a to-scale drawing with a scale bar, both serials copied out in block capitals, both candidate dates written, Ilsa Kern's grandmother's road in the margin, and Gael Senn's witness signature. Kellan asked for it and did not get it.
- **Sera Quill's license sheet for the new run** (Ch 21, read aloud Ch 24): a single page in the cooperative's own book, naming the work, the four hands, the licensed engineer, the anchor, the path, and the return; the return line carries **three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight a month short** in her own hand, with **M. Vale, engineer of record, and the author of the number in the return line** under it. Not filed anywhere. Cannot be entered as a charter condition. **A later writer may not treat it as superseded.**
- **The cooperative's answer on the form** (Ch 28): *not established* in the box, and a paragraph underneath saying the cause lies below the ground supplied and the document that would establish it cannot be obtained by this cooperative. Read at the counter, entered in Perrin Osse's register with the hour.
- **The cooperative's statement of the thirtieth** (Ch 30): **eleven hundred words**, that its own account of the cause is a rumor with a witness in it. Goes out with the form. **This is the document the Ch 46–48 proof is built on, and it is the cooperative's, and it says the cooperative cannot prove what it knows.**
- **The schedule of damage and the bylaw's sixth part** (Ch 28): £60 10, fourteen days, no appeal. A copy is on the well house door.
- **Three section-nine margin notes**, on consecutive pages: the bypass (eleventh), the twenty-seventh (seventeenth), the bypass refused with five names against it (twenty-seventh of the month). All unnumbered, unfunded, signed by nobody but Marek, each ending *this is worth nothing against a cut*.
- **The fieldbook** (Ch 10 onward): in Lissa's custody in the seed house press, under the ledger. It may not be moved, sold, lent, or put in another instrument without telling the sub-office, and Bray will seize it the first time it is opened by an unlicensed hand without a licensed reader in the room. It is due to be produced on the eighteenth.

## Historical locks

- The Quiet Break was an engineered Crown Engine failure concealed as a natural collapse.
- The **Brinewake Collapse** was a Salt March regional failure nineteen years before the present: 4,200 people died in the first winter and 9,000 were displaced. Sera Quill removed the safety relay's red-line warning, falsified its log, and signed a report blaming weather and salt creep.
- Aldren Vale died eight years ago while trying to interrupt the current pruning cycle. He did not survive as a hidden ally.
- The Rootway is adaptive ecological memory, not a deity, single mind, or simple machine.

## Relationship and cost locks

- Marek and Tova's romance is the only approved romance. Their first cooperation is professional and contentious; later intimacy requires informed consent and does not give either person authority over the other.
- Marek's final sacrifice transfers the living resonance of his rootmark into one of the tested public seedhearts, not his mind, and costs him continuous Deep Archive access.
- The earlier Volume 13 public-evidence sacrifice costs Marek one irreplaceable Aldren memory, not all archive access.
- Tova's Volume 08 anchor work leaves partial hearing loss. Lissa's forgiveness remains partial and practical. Halden's reconciliation requires a specific public concession. Tarin is captured, testifies under guard, and is not redeemed.

## Volume 01 plan locks

Approved in `outline/volume-01.md` and `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md`. These are the governing plan, not yet prose. They resolve or specify existing canon and do not change any world rule.

- **Fieldbook fragment.** Aldren's fieldbook is a fragment: front board, living root bead, and a handful of pages. The back board and most of Aldren's survey pages are gone. This specifies `bible/world.md`'s "old fieldbook fragment" and is the reason every reading in Volume 01 is fragmentary and why the Aldren thread cannot be resolved from Lantern Reach.
- **Power ceiling.** Volume 01 runs Stage 0 to Stage 1, matching the series contract. Marek's first skin rootmark does not appear until his first independent graft in Volume 02. The graft his crew performs in Chapters 21–25 is crew work under Sera's license, not his.
- **No anchor work, no romantic turn.** No human living anchor is used in Volume 01, and Marek and Tova reach a conditional alliance with nothing tender in it. The slow burn runs 1–49 as disagreement, not as attraction.
- **Capacity.** Volume 01's root work is local conduit work only: a well, a seed house, and a short run of living pipe with one local steward. No other bridge capacity appears in this volume.
- **The deciding forum.** A Well Directorate hearing at the Fennmere sub-office, before an appointed hearing officer, with Inspector Ansel Bray reporting as inspector and giving evidence rather than chairing. The cooperative cannot appeal it.
- **Volume 01's deeper answer.** What Marek reads in Chapter 6 is a maintenance pattern, and what Sera eventually calls it is a target pattern: a work order written in advance, not a memory of a season that has not happened. This satisfies the Volume 01 row in the series mystery schedule. Nobody in Volume 01 names the Crown Engine, the Continuity Office, False Season, or Iona Vey.
- **Terminology caution.** Kellan's surviving charter is described as a monitored charter with conditions. Do not use "covenant" for it; that word is reserved for the First Continuity covenant in Volume 13.
- **Memory bleed, not sensory bleed.** The Chapter 6 and Chapter 9 costs are a **memory bleed**, per `bible/terminology.md`. Characters may say sensory bleed in dialogue; narration and records use the registered term.
- **Pathhound.** Registered in `bible/terminology.md`. It herds living things off a broken flow, is redirected by changing the flow, is never commanded, and is not solved by killing its anchor. Two appear in Volume 01, at the weir and on the dead flow downstream.
- **The compound pad.** The coat over the wound is a **pad**, not a seal: laid by Kellan's crew four seasons ago as a surface hold over open scarwood, topped up annually, never replaced, and described in the log as a proper seal that was never installed. Day 3 is the last top-up. The hand-built seal in Chapters 14–17 is the first real one, and its failure or survival decides the flood.
- **Kellan's fate.** Exposed by corroborated records plus Sera's licensed read, deprived of the Lantern Reach contract, kept on a private charter under monitored conditions because Fennmere's low quarter needs his water, and turned into a witness. Not killed, not fled, not redeemed.
- **The family.** Lissa Vale is Marek's **mother** and runs the seed house. Nia is his younger sister. There is no third woman in this family; do not write one.
- **Spelling.** The bible uses US spelling: labor, behavior, license, fertilizer. Outline and prose should match.

### Batch 0001 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Charter expiry and annual inspection are both day 18. Ch 1–2 on day 1 · day 2 is Tova's two-day look at the records and carries no chapter · Ch 3 on day 3 afternoon, the closing hours of that look, and Ch 4 that evening · Ch 5 on day 6 · Ch 6 across days 7–8 · Ch 7 on day 9 · Ch 8 and Ch 9 on day 10 · Ch 10 on day 11, with seven days remaining. Ch 3 must land inside the two-day window; moving it later breaks the batch's first irreversible act.

Weekdays follow from the text: day 3 is a Tuesday (Ch 4, Ch 10), so day 6 is a Friday, days 7–8 are a Saturday and a Sunday, day 10 is a Tuesday, day 11 a Wednesday, and the eighteenth a Wednesday. Sera's two-day supervised session therefore runs over a weekend. **Ch 5 (day 6, Friday) carries no weekday name**: it says the cistern job is until dark *today* and that the fieldbook question is hers to raise now rather than at the gate *tomorrow morning*. Ch 4 and Ch 10's "Tuesday" is day 3 and is correct.

Two within-chapter counts are fixed and must not drift: Ch 9 (day 10) ends on **eight** days, and Ch 10 (day 11) on **seven**.

### Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0001

- **One Fieldbook panel in the batch**, in Ch 6, three plain lines, at the moment of choice about the head, and the batch prompt names that chapter.
- Ch 6 contact: supervised, Sera present, a licensed engineer fitted the copper clip, Gael at the sump. Cost is a memory bleed. Sera takes the fieldbook's contents **from the operator by dictation, not from the instrument** — she never opens the book. This must stay true, because Chapter 8's record depends on it.
- Ch 9 contact: deliberate, unsupervised, unlicensed, **no bead and no return**. Cost is a second memory bleed **of the same four seconds as the first and dearer than the first** — a handprint he cannot account for and the hour between the gate and the crown — plus a handprint on the weir stones. Damaged wood gave him a wrong direction and nothing else. **The length is not the price; do not make the second contact longer.**
- Marek ends the batch with nothing on his skin and cannot repeat either contact. Sera has told him to look at his arm twice a week for a year and to record in his own book the first time it is not nothing.
- In Ch 8, after the fieldbook goes on the record, he lays two fingers on the **front board**, well away from the bead, and does not touch the bead. Perrin Osse records that he did not. Do not give him a third contact.

### New locks from the drafted Batch 0001

- **The split** is the point above which the charter holder's private branch leaves the main. Kellan's gauge is on the head tap, above the split, so it can report what the node is giving and never where it goes. This is why six years of readings were plausible and wrong.
- **The copper seam** at the housing, where the collar's ring meets the conduit's ferrule, is the contact point any fieldbook session uses. Fitted nine years ago and never looked at.
- **The Crown sign at Weir Row** is the load-bearing piece of Movement 1 evidence: *BRANCH MAINTENANCE — DISTRICT SCHEDULE. NO LOCAL HOLDER. REPORT CUTS TO THE SUB-OFFICE, FENNMERE.* Notification is not in the schedule as drafted.
- **The fieldbook's impossible date** is written into Finding 2's companion note as *probably a copying error; to be treated as one, and no inference to be drawn from it.* Marek asked for that wording himself. It is the only protection the volume gives him, and Movement 2 may not quietly undo it.
- **Sera has seen the shape of the schedule table before and will not say where.** Her Ch 8 line is the entire payoff of Ch 5's "I have papers I will not show you." On the seventh day she is at the salt cistern until dark and on the eighth she is at the well head; she is not in two places on one day.
- **Kellan is not a villain and not a coward.** He refuses four district instructions to open a well he is paid to hold closed (Sera, Ch 10), and he drove his compound kit up the fen road in a storm to get it out of an inspection. He removed the exclusivity from his second offer and left the accusation in. He is not redeemed.
- **Renn Tallow's refusal** at the weir is one flat line, *"I have got the load,"* and it is never explained or softened. Do not turn it into cruelty.
- **Gael Senn** is the witness who signs in Ch 7, the person at risk in Ch 9, and the one Lissa chooses in Ch 5 because she wants this to be real more than she wants to be important. She is 23 and wants to be a rootwright.
- **Ilsa Kern** is Hansa Kern's granddaughter, on the short road to the weir, and the E. Kern initials on the work book page are a Kern family signature, not a Lissa Vale relation. Do not restate the family connection wrongly.
- **Tova's unread paper** (Ch 4) is a paper she has read on the shape of free offers with a signature attached. It is **not dated, not numbered, and not located** in the prose any more — she refuses to describe it in the room precisely so that nobody in the room starts behaving like the people in the paper. It is a Volume 03 plant. She has not been offered a number, so Brinewake cannot be read forward off this page. Do not restore a number to it.
- **Nia leaves on day 20** and does not return within the volume. Her list of what she takes is to be written with columns for crate, destination, and who stood there when it went, in the cooperative's own book.
- **Tova's evidentiary rule** is now cooperative policy: any reading taken by an unlicensed hand is entered in the seed house book with the date, the instrument, the taker, and the licensed person present, and the entry is open to anybody who asks. The chapter names it. Later writers must not restate it as a Crown rule.
- **Section nine of the charter schedule** allows any entry made by an officer of the cooperative, dated and signed, as evidence of what the cooperative intended and not of what it did. This is the mechanism that carries the bypass as a note. Use it again rather than inventing a second mechanism.

### Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0001 on review, and the locks they created

Recorded here because Batch 0002 was written against the *state* files, not the prose, and four of these were wrong in both.

1. **The two Crown serials live in Marek's weir notebook (Ch 7), not in the fieldbook.** The fieldbook's last page holds only Aldren's line of figures (*Seventeen. Set in the low. Eleven. Watch the fourth beat in the small hours.*), the column head and figure *confirmed — set — 4.*, and the impossible year. Bray reads N-2214 and N-3318 off the to-scale drawing in Ch 8. Do not put a serial on a fieldbook page.
2. **Sera's Ch 8 beat is now on the page.** She asks for the page, reads the two numbers twice, says she has seen the shape of that table, refuses to say where she saw it or what it is, and asks that both refusals be entered with her name. She still names no branch and no office, and she has not been asked about Brinewake. That exchange is the whole payoff of Ch 5.
3. **Both contacts are four seconds.** The second is *dearer*, not longer. Its extra costs are the handprint on the weir stones and the lost hour between the third gate and the crown.
4. **Tova withdraws nothing in Ch 4.** The vault veto stands, the cooperative broke it, and the record says so. There is no withdrawn condition anywhere in the batch.
5. **Pathhound reaches the page.** Lissa Vale names it in Ch 9, says it herds rather than hunts, that it cannot be called off or tied up or be rid of by killing what it stands on, and that the only thing that moves it is a change in where the water goes. She also says the word has not been said in this yard in thirty years. **Rootwoken** is used once, in Marek's recognition of the animal. The second, adult pathhound is Chs 23–25 and is a different animal.
6. **The compost return link is planted in Ch 9, not Ch 10.** Ilsa Kern says it at dawn, in the pit, immediately after the compound kit. Tova's arithmetic in Ch 10 is confirmation, not revelation. Do not spend this connection anywhere else.
7. **Ch 4 records two dispositions and one defeat.** Motion one (the trays and the trust's material) carried 34–0. Motion two (entering the claim in the log) **lost 9–22 with 4 abstentions**, of thirty-five hands in a room of forty, because the room would rather be sealed by Kellan's hand on the eighteenth than by its own on the fourteenth. Dunmar will not enter a true number without a licensed cause. The open book is therefore a *private act of the meeting*, not a filing, and it is the reason Ch 8's open book is a document and not a finding.
8. **The refusal becomes a carried motion in Marek's name.** Lissa refuses it in the room, then demands it be entered as a motion; Marek moves it himself so that a seed steward's refusal is not the operative act. Carried 31–4, with Dunmar entering the number, the hour, and the fact that no member signed Oskell's second page.
9. **The compost price reconciles.** Bought compost is 4 s/cwt. Sera's two hundred hundredweight on the seventh day is £40 and she pays for it out of her own money and enters it in the cooperative's open book under her own name, so the open book opens on a document that is not the cooperative's. Tova reads the water-meadow farm's invoice off that entry in Ch 10. The £14 fee and the £5 11 treasury are untouched.
10. **The mill board is a record of return, not of draw.** It counts wheel turns on milling days only — two or three in a week — and 261 is the highest single day on it. 720 turns/day is the whole holding's stated absorption in a hot week, every acre including the outer rows, which the mill never sees the water from. They are different quantities and must not be set on the same line of the same page. Both are locked; neither is a lie. This matters because the mill tally is one of the four records at the Ch 44–46 climax.
11. **The dial and the stroke tally are separate instruments throughout.** The dial is pressure; the tally is volume; the recorded result is *one hundred and thirty-four turns of the handle, needle at three-eighths under the mark*, and it is a rate, not a day. The daily figures (1,100 / 720 / 380 / 832,000) are the log's, not the test's. Do not let a later chapter make a ten-minute hand test into a daily turn count.
12. **The pulse has a custodian.** Bray times it twice in the open pit and does not write it, because he did not feel it and would not put an officer's pen to a thing one man felt and an inspector did not. Sera's Ch 6 wording (*no inference is to be drawn from it*) is the authority for that discretion. Nobody names it.
13. **Ch 3's beat is shame, not relief.** He respects a refusal in the moment that counts and does not agree with it, and the discovery that the difference is the whole of what he has to learn is the chapter's turn. Relief is named, taken, turned over, and refused.
14. **Ch 1 delivers the pump.** Marek clears a fouled foot valve and gland by hand, runs the pit with the collar off, and fills the dry yard trough. Four men drink from it and the water tastes of iron. That is the pride beat and it is why the dread lands. The counter stays at 41/48 and he says why out loud.

### Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied

Nothing in Batch 0001 required a change to `bible/`. Two small additions were made to the world by drafting and are recorded here as approved-by-use rather than as a new rule:

1. **Bought compost at 4 shillings a hundredweight**, and the seed vault's return of 400 cwt/month. This is a price, not a power rule, and it is what makes the lost flock irreversible in Ch 20–30 and Ch 49.
2. **Ker(n) family on the fen**: Hansa Kern, 1802, and the short road to the weir. Local detail only.

Batch 0002 added two more of the same kind, recorded the same way:

3. **The charter's fourth part.** The schedule has a fourth part, first clause (emergency repair by unlicensed hands, from Ch 12) and **second clause (records made at the hour of the work)**, which is the rule the whole batch turns on. It is a charter provision, not a Crown Engine mechanism, and it is never to be confused with anything from later volumes.
4. **The district works schedule.** A district-level, six-column, ninety-row printed extract with no headings, issued automatically with every renewal application. It is *not* a Crown-wide table. **It shows one district, and the courier's three other provinces are not in it.** Nobody has seen a whole table and nobody in this volume will.

### New locks from the drafted Batch 0002

- **The cooperative holds nothing after midnight on the eighteenth.** It has no right to draw from the head, to work on it, or to enter anything about it in a district book. The pump still runs because nobody has sealed it, and that is the only reason the seed house has water. **Any report from anybody, including a neighbor who tastes the water and finds iron, reopens the emergency clause.** This is the siege and it must remain the background condition of Movements 3 and 4.
- **The load test choice is spent.** Marek tested at 1,100 and not at 720, on the record, and it is not a decision to be re-litigated. The seal has now been proven at the full logged draw and the finding is `not a leak`.
- **The seal's weakness is placed.** The six o'clock corner, the same place the pad always wept from, is where it weeps. It is a weep now, and **a quarter of an inch is the measured width**; **half an inch is where Sera stops calling it a weep.** And **a leak under sacking at full draw goes into the housing and not into the pit.** Do not resolve this before Ch 41. Do not write this lock as *a finger's width is a leak* — that phrasing is the inverted form and it was corrected in fix 11 below.
- **The seal is a holding repair, not a Crown seal, and Sera says so in a district file.** A Crown pad cannot be cut out and looked at; withies can. This is adverse to a Crown practice and is the first document of its kind in the file. It is Movement 4 and climax material, not something to spend early.
- **Kellan's letter (Ch 18) is undelivered and in a cart.** Marek will not ask him to reduce his draw, will not ask for a return, will not speak about an unmeasured log in public, and will not say the acceptance column was a lie. Nia Vale is carrying it to Fennmere by hand on the twentieth. **She may hand it over or she may not; if it is delivered, it is the first cooperative document Kellan has ever been sent, and it is how he becomes a willing witness in Ch 44–46.**
- **The twenty-seventh is a live clock.** N-2214/K is scheduled to be executed on the twenty-seventh, a Friday, seven days after Nia leaves. The cooperative is the local holder of the node, is not the holder of the branch, cannot object, and was not notified. Movement 3 must decide what the cooperative does about a cut it owes and did not order.
- **Dunmar's *none known*.** The charter clerk has put a statement into a district filing that was true when he wrote it and false by noon the same day, and has entered the fact in the margin. It is a live second technicality and Bray has so far not asked about it. It may be used once and only once, and it belongs to a later chapter than the eighteenth.
- **The bypass note and the schedule note are both section-nine margin notes**, unnumbered, unfunded, dated, and signed by nobody but Marek, and they sit on consecutive pages. The third one, if there is a third, is the same instrument. Do not invent a new mechanism for a plan nobody can fund.
- **Anneke Roos's rule is cooperative policy, and it is the rule that beat them.** *An entry is made at the hour it describes. If it is made late, the lateness is part of the entry.* It is written on the inside cover of the standing pressure log, attributed to her, and it was written on the fourteenth and broken on the seventeenth. The record of the standing log's inside cover must stay consistent wherever the log is read again.
- **Marek has been refused the pit and has accepted it.** Sera's Ch 11 speech is the batch's argument against him and it is not to be softened or repeated. If a later writer needs him near a living thing, the licensed reader is in the room and he is outside the housing.
- **Gael Senn is the monthly examiner** and her name is the second on the certificate. This is the seed of the climax; do not reassign it, and do not let her be rescued from it — she is the one who has to write *nothing found* honestly in a bad month.
- **Sera's liability is now public in two documents**: the certificate (her name first) and her Ch 17 statement putting a Crown seal specification into a district file as a thing to be compared against, at her own professional risk. She has not been asked about Brinewake and has not offered it. Her third refusal on the twentieth is the same two refusals. **Do not spend her confession here.**
- **Nia is gone and does not return within the volume.** What she leaves behind is the first monthly root-change report, which is written in her hand with her name under the figures, and it goes to Cray and to the sub-office. Her absence has to work against Marek through Movements 3–5.
- **The compost arithmetic is unchanged and unpaid.** 4 s/cwt, 400 cwt/month, £80/month against £5 11, and the levy took £12 6 of the households' money that was not compost money. The bed is losing heat; the trays go to the mill floor twice a day under sacking. This is the mechanism of the Ch 23–25 reversal and it must not be spent anywhere else.
- **Marda Vole is a person, not a network.** She mends chain, she was paid in bread, she will not be asked about anything, and she has not been in this fen since the sixteenth. The Undercommons in this volume is one courier, one warning and one piece of procedure. Never a rescue, never a second one, and never a name above the office that does not exist.

### Batch 0002 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Ch 11 day 12 (Thu) · Ch 12 day 13 (Fri) · Ch 13 day 14 (Sat) · Ch 14 day 15 (Sun) · Ch 15 day 16 (Mon) · Ch 16 day 17 (Tue) · Ch 17 day 18 (Wed, a Wednesday, as locked) · Ch 18 day 19 (Thu) · Ch 19 day 20 (Fri) · Ch 20 day 20, dusk.

Because **day 3 is a Tuesday**, the working days fall as: day 14 is a Saturday, day 15 a Sunday, day 16 a Monday. **The seal was laid on the sixteenth, a Monday, in one working day**, and **the twenty-seventh is a Friday.** Day 3 remains the only fixed weekday anchor for the whole volume; the eighteenth being a Wednesday is locked and correct.

Within-batch counts: Ch 11 ends on six days, Ch 12 on five, Ch 13 on four, Ch 14 on three, Ch 15 on two, Ch 16 on the eve of the eighteenth. Ch 20 ends seven days before the twenty-seventh.

### Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0002

- **Two contacts behind him, unchanged.** No third, no rootmark, no anchor work, no bridge above local conduit, no seedheart, no caretaker link. The Chapter 20 reading is Sera Quill's, and the panel belongs to the instrument in her hands.
- Sera has checked his arm three times — the eighth, the twelfth and the twentieth — and it is still nothing. She has now said the *look at it twice a week* instruction three times and has promised not to say it a fourth.
- **One Fieldbook panel in the batch**, in Ch 19, three plain lines, at the moment of the load choice, in the book, in a licensed reader's hands.
- The Chapter 20 procedure in the book is **cut, wait, cut**. Sera says it is a procedure and refuses to say whose. This is the first time the fieldbook's content has been correctly identified in the prose, and it is still short of the Ch 46 answer, which is *a work order written in advance*. Do not collapse the two.
- **The pulse has now been logged four times** — the Ch 1 note, the Ch 6 finding, the Ch 12 first-page entry, and the Ch 19 count of seven and eight beats continuing after the pump stopped — always with the phrase **cause not established** and never with a name. Nobody in Volume 01 may name it or explain it.

### Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0002, and the locks they created

**There were two passes over this batch, and they are not the same kind of review.** The first was the writer checking its own work; it produced the fifteen items numbered 1–15 below. The second was an independent review pass, whose findings are in `reviews/batch-0002.findings.md`. **Items 6 and 12 were claimed by the first pass as fixed and were not true of the prose until the second pass corrected them**, and item 16 below is recorded as *not* a fix because it was the first pass's own arithmetic being wrong. Read this section knowing which pass produced which claim. The prose governed wherever it was right; the state files were corrected wherever the prose was right and the file was wrong.

1. **The compound pad is four coats, not five.** Ch 1's prose (*"three more coats"*) and Ch 1's own notebook line agree on a base coat plus three dressings. Ch 9 read *"the base coat and the four dressings"* and this file carried the same figure. **Ch 9 is corrected to three dressings** and the table above is corrected. Four governs. Ch 14's four-layer strip depends on it. **This is the one edit this batch made to a chapter inside an already-completed and already-reviewed batch; `reviews/batch-0001.findings.md` has been amended to record it**, so that the archived findings for Batch 0001 and the prose of Batch 0001 agree.
2. **Row forty-one holds four dates and a marked hold, not five dates.** Sera's column schema (item; order date; three execution columns; and a hold in the fifth) leaves four date slots, and the row is dated to match. **The fifth figure is a hold that is also marked, and nobody in the room can tell which figure is which.** This is better than the original and must not be re-simplified.
3. **Ch 12's closing count is five days, not six.** Day 13 to the eighteenth is five. Ch 11 is six. The chain is now six, five, four, three, two, eve.
4. **The twenty-seventh is ten days from the seventeenth and nine from the eighteenth.** Ch 16 says ten; Ch 17 and Ch 20 say nine and seven. **Day 27 is a Friday.**
5. **Two meta intrusions are gone**: the phrase *the Ch 4 formula* in the narration, and *the only document in this volume* in Ch 18. The Ch 18 letter is presented as sealed, written, and read by one person who was not asked, and it is **undelivered**.
6. **The levy is tallied one way.** *(Claimed by the first pass and not true of the prose until the independent review corrected it — see `reviews/batch-0002.findings.md`, finding 2. The figures below are the delivered ones.)* Sixteen households. **Fourteen signatures, nine of them putting a round share in.** The other five of the fourteen put nothing in the line: two of them put nothing at all and both were people Marek had taught to read a dial, one was Hesk's daughter with a shilling and *and I am sorry*, and the last two signed because they did not want to be the ones who had not, with no reason anybody could write down. **Two of the sixteen never signed, and their names are not recorded**, because the paper forbade it. £12 6, plus the shilling counted and entered separately and not added in. **£1 14 short, made up by Marek.** The fourthpence in Ch 15 is the mill boy's and is entered separately too. Any later chapter that tallies the levy must reproduce these numbers exactly.
7. **Ch 14's unspent promise is gone.** The narrator no longer promises an objection three days later that never happens. In its place: the working-notes entry for the fifteenth, with the licensed person named, and Sera coming down the ladder to help haul Byre up.
8. **The Ch 20 reading is reconciled with Finding 3.** The eighth gave valve, hand, wait, valve, lamp, valve; the twentieth gave cut, wait, cut. **Sera states the difference herself, in front of witnesses, before anybody else can**, and the explanation she offers is that a fieldbook reads what a node has recently had done to it. **Finding 3's wording and Ch 6's *four things* are untouched.** A fourth line goes into the cooperative's book: *a licensed reader at the same seam did not obtain the sequence obtained on the eighth; both are logged; the difference is unaccounted for.*
9. **The date-stake no longer knows the future.** Five names, no sixth line, and the prose says explicitly that nothing on it says what comes next. **The five are the four hands in the pit plus Sera Quill**, and the fifth is cut in a different hand than the other four because she stood over the whole working day and wanted to be on the wood. Do not write the fifth name as an absence. *(Corrected by the independent review; the earlier draft said the fifth name was not Marek's, which read as though Marek had not worked it.)*
10. **The certificate has three names and Marek signs it**, witnessed, with Lissa entering the hour.
11. **The weep is a quarter of an inch**, measured with a rule before the sentence is spoken, and **half an inch is where Sera stops calling it a weep.** The thumb-width/finger-width inversion is gone. The ten o'clock check finds the same quarter inch with the load off.
12. **The day clock is clean throughout.** Day 12 Thursday, 13 Friday, 14 Saturday, 15 Sunday, 16 Monday, 17 Tuesday, 18 Wednesday, 19 Thursday, 20 Friday, 27 Friday. Ch 13's hours were rebuilt around a ten o'clock start with the bailing running eight to a quarter past ten, and the refused entry is the **second hour, eleventh of the clock**. **No record in the batch is dated to a named month** — *February* appears only as a foreseeable month ahead, never as the month a document is dated in, and Ch 1's *Michaelmas* governs, so no three-way conflict can form. *(The first pass wrote that no month is named anywhere in the batch. That was false — February appears thirteen times across eight chapters — and the accurate claim is the narrower one above.)* **Row forty-one's second execution is seven days before the seventeenth**, because the cut was made on the night of the tenth; do not write eleven.
13. **The mill tally board is untouched.** The standing pressure log lives on the well-house bench, the levy paper goes on the well-house door, and Nia writes the monthly report on the back of a seed order on the seed-house table. **The tally board across the race is still the mill's, and is one of the four records at the climax.**
14. **Sockets and hasps are different things.** The four withies go into sockets; Marda Vole mends the mill sluice chain and the well-house hasp, and explicitly refuses the four eyes in the frame because she would not know what to tell anybody to hang there.
15. **The works extract.** Perrin Osse's prohibition is now *you may not keep it, and any line you write is yours and a clerk can find it* — and **Dunmar says so out loud, and then carries the number anyway**, which is the beat the earlier draft was missing.
16. **A finding the first pass raised in error and which is therefore *not* a fix:** 200 cwt at 4 s/cwt is 800 shillings, which is **£40**, not £8. Ch 6's compost arithmetic and the £40 it puts against three fees are correct, as is 400 cwt at 4 s/cwt = £80 a month.

### New locks from the drafted Batch 0003

- **The graft in Chapters 21–25 is crew work under Sera Quill's license and Marek is not in the ditch.** He says so at the top of the ladder on the twenty-second, in front of the four, and Sera confirms she will take him out by the collar. He holds the boards, reads the dial, and says numbers. **His first independent graft and his first skin rootmark are still Volume 02 and nothing in this batch moves them.**
- **The graft is a *local conduit* and nothing above that.** Thirty-one yards of new ditch, a fiber bed, four willow sets, a physical conduit touching living flow. It is not a seed-route bridge and the words are not used. Volume 01's capacity ceiling is unchanged.
- **The three exchange requirements are named in the license sheet and the third one is empty.** Anchor: willow, four sets, off the south embankment windbreak. Path: the new run and its fiber bed and its lashings. Return: labor, recorded, and one hundredweight of compost against four hundred specified. **The license sheet is a single page and it is a licensed act and an arithmetic deficit on the same page, in Sera Quill's hand, her name first.** A later writer may not quietly mend it.
- **The windbreak has been cut for about a hundred feet and will not be a windbreak there in a year.** This is the cooperative's own thinning, and it makes the Crown's controlled-burn offer in Movement 4 worse. Do not have anyone forget that they started it.
- **The south embankment windbreak still stands in the spring of Movement 4.** Nothing in this batch burns it.
- **The compost mechanism is spent here and may not be spent anywhere else.** The loop is closed on the page in Ch 24: labor → mill floor → seed bed → bin → flock. One hole, in the middle, and the hole is the tenth of last month. The £80 a month, the 400 cwt, the 4 s and the £5 11 are unchanged and Tova's arithmetic is confirmation, not revelation, for the third time.
- **The Weir Row branch and the low quarter's branch are the same branch.** That is the load-bearing geography of Movement 3 and it is now stated in the prose (Ch 26). A Crown crew cut it two hundred yards from Lantern Reach on the twenty-seventh and nine miles upstream of forty-one households. **Do not later describe them as separate branches.**
- **The second pathhound is one animal in two places and it is not the weir animal.** Weir animal: Ch 9, on a crown, herding 130 head, walked off down a drainage channel. Fennmere animal: an old collie, one ear off, holding a dead flow in the low quarter on the twenty-sixth and standing in nine inches of moving water in the Weir Row channel on the afternoon of the twenty-seventh. It moved because **a channel that has had no water in it for five years had water in it that morning**, which is Lissa Vale's rule working, and which is the only thing in this batch that pays it off. **It is not commanded, not killed, not tied up, and not made into a symbol.** Joss Pyle brings it milk. The weir animal does not return.
- **The Crown's own schedule is structurally unavailable, and that is a decision rather than a villain.** Perrin Osse states on the counter, off the register, that the district will not produce the works extract to anybody, because the table carries a column saying whether the work is Crown-held and producing it is an admission the district does not make to a body holding no charter. **Nobody hid anything. That is the point.** Movement 4 and the hearing must not overturn this without a reason the reader can follow.
- **Dunmar's *none known* has been priced and deliberately not spent.** Ch 26 and Ch 28 both name it as the reason the schedule cannot be used, and both times the cooperative declines to use the schedule rather than detonate the box. **It is still live, still to be used once only, and it is now a cost the characters are carrying on purpose.**
- **The open book is still not a filing, and it is also unread.** Twenty-six days, nobody came. This is a fact about the world, not a punishment, and it must not be turned into a crowd scene in a later batch.
- **The batch's one Fieldbook panel is spent**, in Ch 24, three plain lines, in Sera's hands, at the copper seam, read aloud to four witnesses: *The new run is fed. / The new run is fed from below. / Below is not yours.* Sera refuses to say whether it is a description or an instruction, forbids the phrase *the book told us to* in any document, and adds a fourth refusal of her own about it. **No second panel until a later batch, and it must be one.** Nobody in Volume 01 may name what is written into the wood.
- **The public-proof rule is stated, and it has two parts and a price.** A second licensed reader on the same node through her own anchor on her own date without being told what the first read, and dated records a clerk can take away and hold. **The cure is unsatisfiable this month because the Assembly's notice period is fourteen days and the representations are due in eleven working days — not because of money.** The second reader is **Idris Vane**, and he is the same instrument the climax will need.
- **The seal is untouched.** A quarter of an inch, twice measured on the twenty-first, still a quarter inch on the thirtieth, at the idle, not a leak. Do not resolve it before Ch 41 and do not let it be quietly mended. The second law of the seal — a leak under sacking at full draw goes into the housing and not into the pit — is unused and remains available for the flood.
- **The pressure pulse is now logged six times**, not four: the Ch 1 note, the Ch 6 finding, the Ch 12 first-page entry, the Ch 19 counts, **the Ch 21 morning count at the coping stone and the Ch 23 return at the rail**, and the Ch 23 full-draw run of nine. Always with **cause not established**, never with a name, never in a conclusion. **Nobody in Volume 01 may name or explain it.**
- **The siege got more expensive and nobody resolved it.** The reported event on the twenty-fourth reopened the node file *and* recalled the referred renewal application. Bray is still charter officer through the end of the year; the file does not travel with him; the emergency clause is live and none of the three things has been done. **This is the background condition of Movement 4.**
- **Bray was moved by a document and the document was the cooperative's own honesty, and it moved him against them.** He read three extracts of their own book out loud at a counter and said the shortfall line was the one that finishes them, because a body that does not know what it is doing does not write that down. **He never becomes friendly and he is never outsmarted, and in Ch 28 he refuses them advice because advice would be worth nothing in a cold room and they would not have earned it.**
- **Renn Tallow's refusal is not softened and is not referred to.** He appears once, in Ch 26, in a mill head race, says one flat true thing about the branch, and adds that he has not come to make the weir gate a thing and would like that recorded. Nobody records it. He does not apologize.
- **Kellan's letter is still undelivered and still unresolved.** Nia is ten days gone. Nothing in this batch reports on it, and Movement 4 must not settle it by report.
- **Tova's refusal is arithmetically correct and neither of them is rescued.** She refuses in advance, at a table, with the sum; he looks for a fourth option and kills all three himself; he lets it stand; he enters it with the lateness declared; she initials it and reads it aloud, which is not her habit, and does not look at him. **Her second line — that refusing him is cheap for her, and that the second time is easier — is the movement's best sentence and it must not be softened in any later summary.**
- **The cost that cannot be entered is the movement's structural point.** The three acres are not the cooperative's, so they are not in its book, not in the charge, and not anybody else's problem. Lissa Vale says it out loud at the seed house door: everything anybody lost this month went into a book with a name against it, and the only thing Marek lost that was his own could not go anywhere.
- **The cooperative's standing relationship to the fieldbook has changed by one degree.** Lissa says **no once** on the twenty-fourth where she said no twice on the twentieth, and names the change herself and refuses it to be confused with forgiveness. Keep that exact.
- **Sera's arm check is a fourth-time nothing and she does not nag.** The *twice a week* instruction is spent. Do not say it again.

### Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0003 on review

A review pass was run on Batch 0003 (`logs/batch-0003.review.log`) and its fourteen numbered findings were applied in place. The batch was **not** restarted, no scene was replaced, and no planned plot was changed: the graft as licensed crew work, the compost reversal, the un-built bypass, the five refusals, Tova's refusal, the schedule of damage and the proof rule are all as drafted. The fixes are listed here in the same style as the two earlier batches, because a later writer needs to know which figures and dates were touched and which were not.

1. **Ch 30 numbered a refusal while saying she had not numbered it, and the number was wrong.** Sera's *third* refusal contradicted Ch 21's *my three refusals stand where they are* and the state files' **fourth**. The number and the false count are both out. The line now says the refusal *is a different one from the other three and I have not given it a number*, which is what the state files assert and what her characterization requires. **Sera Quill has four refusals in the volume and the fourth is unnumbered and unexplained.**
2. **Ch 29's refusal entry was dated three incompatible ways in five lines.** It claimed the draw was *proposed at the first hour of the twentieth* — a day that is **before the chapter** — while the table scene was the same evening as her own *a sitting at a table on a Sunday evening* and the entry itself said *the eighth hour of the same day*. The entry now names the two events separately and truthfully: proposed at the seed house table **on the Sunday evening** in the presence of T. Reed, put to S. Quill at the pit **on the Wednesday morning**, and written **at the eighth hour of the day after the Wednesday**, with the lateness still declared. Two supporting labels were corrected with it: the table scene is the Sunday evening, not the Monday, and the shortcut he thought about for four minutes was the Wednesday, not the Tuesday. **No number in the entry changed.** 380 turns/day, four days, the ninth page, both grounds, both refusals standing, and the one-day lateness are all as drafted.
3. **Ch 28's Anneke Roos argued from a false date.** She claimed the hundredweight went in on the twenty-first of *last* month; **it went in on the twenty-first, which is the same day the schedule's damage window opens.** Her argument is now the stronger and arithmetically true one: a hundredweight is **a quarter of one month's return** against a main that has had **nothing** put into it since the middle of October, because the flock went on the tenth and did not come back. **A reviewer's suggested alternative figure of *twenty days* between compost and wells was not adopted: on this batch's own calendar the compost and Slee's well are the same day, so that figure is wrong. The quarter-of-a-month argument replaces it and every number in it (100 of 400 cwt, the middle of October, the tenth) is already locked.**
4. **Ch 27 dated the Fennmere collie to the wrong weekday.** The low-quarter walk was the **sixth of the month, a Thursday** (day 26), not Wednesday. Corrected.
5. **Ch 26's written note said *six of us* for a party of five.** Marek's plan, Renn Tallow's count, and the chapter's own last line all say five. Corrected to *five of us*.
6. **Day counts taken from the tenth were wrong in four places, not two.** The seam was opened on the night of the tenth, so the counts to the twenty-fourth, the twenty-sixth and the twenty-seventh are **fourteen**, **sixteen** and **seventeen**. Corrected: Ilsa Kern carrying it *fourteen days* (Ch 24), the second die cut *sixteen days ago* (Ch 26), and the bleed *seventeen days* in all three of its occurrences in Ch 27. **Ch 27 already used *seventeen days before* for the same anchor two pages earlier, which is what fixed the figure.** The bleed row in the table above is corrected to seventeen to match.
7. ~~**The month boundary was applied wrongly across the whole batch.**~~ **This fix was itself built on a wrong boundary and the Batch 0004 review has partly reversed it. Read the correction before using anything in it.** The first half of it was right and stands: days 1–20 are the tail of October and calling them *this month* was an error. **The second half was wrong**: it held that days 21–30 are the *first … the tenth* of a calendar month, and it duly rewrote about thirty places in Chs 21, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29 and 30 to agree — including three quoted records (the Ch 24 use-log line, the entry Bray reads out in Ch 28, and Sera's opening in Ch 30), Teale's Ch 23 and Ch 24 letters, and Marek's Ch 26 working note. **Days 21–30 are the twenty-first … the thirtieth of October. Every one of those thirty edits has now been undone and replaced with the plain number**, and the day numbers and weekdays were never touched. **The rule is now in the table under *Batch 0003 day clock*, and no day in 21–30 may be called the first … the tenth of anything.** No season day number was changed by either pass and no month is named in a document.
8. **Ch 26 carried a garbled token**, *had the gate barrowseated*. Now *had the gate barred and seated*, which is what a mill head race gate is.
9. **Ch 28 contained the batch's only metafictional intrusion**, *in the voice of a man who has learned what that means from a chapter that is not in this book.* The clause is now in-world — *in the voice of a man who has learned what that phrase is for* — and nothing else in the sentence moved.
10. **Ch 30's proof-rule clause implied Marek had taken licensed readings.** *the only licensed reading that has ever been taken on this node by anybody but Mr Vale* contradicted Sera's own *a rumor with a witness in it* about his four seconds in the pit and the fact that he is Stage 0 with no license. It now reads: the only licensed reading ever taken on this node, and **Mr Vale has never taken one, because he is not licensed to take one and has never said that he was.** The argument the clause serves — a reading is public, dated, and therefore the district's — is unchanged.
11. **Ch 24's witness count was one short.** Sera addressed *the three of you who are standing there* with four named standing there and an entered form reading *four witnesses present*. Now *the four of you who are standing there*, which also correctly leaves Tova Reed out, since she is on the boards behind them. **The panel budget is unaffected: still one panel, three lines, four witnesses.**
12. **Ch 25 conflated the water company with the district.** Slee's man with a stick is *the water company at Fennmere* in his own account and in Ch 26; only the later line called him *a man from the district*. Corrected to the water company, and the day corrected with him — the company's second visit followed the second Tarn well, which went off on the twenty-fourth, so **on Tuesday**, not on the Wednesday Slee was describing.
13. **A day-3 back-reference also appeared in Ch 27** (*on the third day of the month*) and was corrected with the rest of fix 7. Recorded separately only because it is the same phrase the review named in Ch 24 and Ch 29 and could be missed on a later pass.
14. **Two state-file claims were wrong and are corrected here:** the mill-wheel row's *126 (Wednesday, month day minus 6)* — day 22 − 6 is a Monday, and 126 was read on **day 18**, the Wednesday before Friday's 131 on day 20 — and the *three chapters over 3,200* in `state/current.md`, which is **four**: Ch 27 (3,226), Ch 28 (3,335), Ch 29 (3,247), Ch 30 (3,741), so the batch is one chapter over the prompt's once-and-only-once allowance for a long chapter. Also corrected here: *Renn Tallow's gate refusal has now been twenty days old* (it was the tenth, and this is the thirtieth), and *Nia is ten days gone*.
15. **The state files twice claimed that no month is named anywhere in Chapters 1–30, and it is false.** The first false claim was *November* in the table above and was removed by the writer. The second was the sentence *no month is named in the prose of Chapters 1–30*, written by the same pass. **October is named four times — Ch 24 twice, Ch 28 once, Ch 29 once — always in speech, never in a document, and it is the name of the previous month, which is what makes the flock's departure *the tenth of October*.** The row has been rewritten to say what is actually true, and the batch-0004 prompt's matching instruction has been corrected with it. **October is now load-bearing: do not contradict it, do not name the month days 21–30 fall in, and do not attach a month to a record.** A fifth calendar slip fell out of the same sweep: Ch 24's *who brought it in before the tenth of this month* is now *of last month*, since day 10 is in the previous month.

### One known defect in Batch 0003 that was found on this pass and deliberately NOT fixed

**Ch 29 narrates days 29–34 while Ch 30 is day 30.** The chapter opens on the Sunday the three acres go pale, then carries the reader through Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday and Friday — the Tova table scene on the Sunday evening, the shortcut Marek thinks about and the body he offers on the Wednesday, the writing of the entry on the Thursday, the three acres coming up sour *on the Friday*. Friday of that week is season day 34, and Ch 30 is fixed to the thirtieth by its own prose, by Sera's fourth arm check, and by the form going out inside fourteen days of the twenty-eighth. **The two chapters therefore overlap, and Ch 30 describes a crust on the three acres that Ch 29 dates to the Wednesday.** **Batch 0004 did not close it and was not asked to.** It used the version the phase prompt recommended: on days 31 and 40 the three acres are described as *the color of a wet road* with **the crust that came up in one sheet on the Sunday and has not moved since**, which is the state the ground was in on the twenty-ninth and does not depend on the Wednesday or the Friday. Ch 30's date, her fourth arm check, the eleven-hundred-word statement and the form's fourteen days all still stand, and nothing in Batch 0004 requires the overlap to be resolved. **The defect is therefore still open and is recorded as open.** This was not among the review's fourteen findings and it is a chapter-structure problem, not a slip: closing it means either re-dating Ch 30 or compressing a week of Ch 29's decline into a single day, and both change the shape of a chapter the reviewer otherwise passed as strong. **It is recorded here rather than fixed, and the next writer must resolve it in prose before Batch 0004's chapters are treated as final** — the cheapest resolution is to keep Ch 29's Sunday-to-Friday span and re-dating Ch 30's three-acres paragraph to the crust already visible on the Sunday, since the crust is what Marek has been walking out into since the twenty-ninth. Nothing in Ch 30's date, her arm check, the statement, or the form's deadline may move.

### Batch 0003 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Ch 21 day 21 (Sat, **the twenty-first**) · Ch 22 day 22 (Sun) · Ch 23 day 23 (Mon) · Ch 24 day 24 (Tue) · Ch 25 day 25 (Wed) · Ch 26 day 26 (Thu) · Ch 27 day 27 (**Fri, the execution**) · Ch 28 day 28 (Sat) · Ch 29 day 29 (Sun) · Ch 30 day 30 (Mon, **the last day of the month**).

This follows from the locked Batch 0002 chain (day 3 a Tuesday, day 18 a Wednesday, day 20 a Friday, day 27 a Friday) and is **the only day numbering a later writer may use for this stretch**.
