# BATCH 0005 — CHAPTERS 41–49, THE VOLUME CLOSE

**Volume 01, Movement 5, *The Long Water*, and the resolution of the volume. Days 41–52, with day 61 named as a live clock and not reached.** Nine chapters. **The batch was reviewed by hand after drafting and every finding was applied in the prose; the findings are in `reviews/batch-0005.findings.md` and the record of what changed is in *The Batch 0005 review pass* at the end of this section.**

## Batch 0005 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Ch 41 day 41 (**Fri, the eleventh**) · Ch 42 day 42 (**Sat, the twelfth**) · Ch 43 days 43–44 (**Sun the thirteenth, Mon the fourteenth**) · Ch 44 day 45 (**Tue, the fifteenth**) · Ch 45 day 46 (**Wed, the sixteenth**) · Ch 46 days 47–48 (**Thu the seventeenth, Fri the eighteenth**) · Ch 47 days 49–50 (**Sat the nineteenth, Sun the twentieth**) · Ch 48 day 51 (**Mon, the twenty-first**) · Ch 49 day 52 (**Tue, the twenty-second**).

**Days 1–30 are October and day 31 is the first of the month after it, so days 41–52 are the eleventh to the twenty-second of that month, and day 61 is the first of the month after that.** The weekdays follow from the locked Batch 0004 chain (day 40 a Thursday) and are the only weekday statement in this batch: the eleventh a Friday, the twelfth a Saturday, the thirteenth a Sunday, the fourteenth a Monday, the fifteenth a Tuesday, the sixteenth a Wednesday, the seventeenth a Thursday, the eighteenth a Friday, the nineteenth a Saturday, the twentieth a Sunday, the twenty-first a Monday, the twenty-second a Tuesday. **No month name appears in any document in Chapters 41–49. October appears in speech and narration only and is always the previous month** — the flock went up the fen road on the tenth of October, the compost return ran on the mill floor from the middle of October, and Nia Vale's cart left on the twentieth of October. February and January are not used in this batch. *March* in this batch is never a month: it is the Salt March, the March road, the March's counting house, or the Dryfoot Trust, as established in Batches 0003 and 0004.

## The day-to-chapter map actually used

The phase prompt supplied a workable map and required the one actually used to be recorded. It was used substantially as given, with two deliberate departures, both recorded here:

| Chapter | Days | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 41 | 41 | the eleventh. The reed gauge's first figure in any book, no baseline, the reason the full-draw load is not applied, Ivo Tarrant turns the four men round at the gate, Sera Quill comes up the road |
| 42 | 42 | the twelfth. The rain begins. The representations and the sixty pounds ten fall due. The March pays for the reading |
| 43 | 43–44 | the thirteenth and fourteenth. The rise in the low ground of Fennmere on the second night of the rain. **The weep becomes a leak.** The second levy. The compost goes in on the fourteenth, not the first |
| 44 | 45 | the fifteenth. The money to Idris Vane, eleven miles and a causeway under. The water reaches the low corner. The four facts at the table |
| 45 | 46 | the sixteenth. The crest. **Marek's six-word sentence.** The corner opened by hand. The design fails at the seventh hour and the night's work is the same hole made bigger and the old channel to Weir Row rodded out |
| 46 | 47–48 | the seventeenth and the eighteenth. **The climax.** Weir Row, the four records, the use log read aloud, the panel, the licensed finding, the work order, the docket, the name, and Vane's figures on the eighteenth |
| 47 | 49–50 | the nineteenth and the twentieth. Renn Tallow's statement recorded, thirty-nine days late. Perrin Osse reconciles the certified copy. A collie at the gateway. The low quarter maps its own line. **The letter comes back sealed, by hand, and Nia does not come back at all** |
| 48 | 51 | the twenty-first, Monday. The sitting. The instrument produced on a form. Vane's reading on the file. The testimony. The conditional charter |
| 49 | 52 | the twenty-second. The resolution and the final image |

**Departures from the prompt's suggested map, both recorded rather than made silently.** First, the prompt's map put the fourth-hour reed-gauge reading in Ch 41 and the last dry day there too; that held. Second, the prompt's map put *the crest and the choice* in Ch 45 (the sixteenth) and *the climax and the reading* in Ch 46 (the seventeenth to the eighteenth); that held. The one thing the prose moved is the **schedule of a single night inside Ch 45**: the corner is opened on the morning of the sixteenth, and at the ninth hour of the evening of the sixteenth the same hole is made bigger and a hundred and forty yards of old channel and a culvert are rodded out, so that Ch 46 can open on the fourth hour of the morning of the seventeenth with the channel running and the branch standing up out of its silt. Chapters do not map one-to-one onto days and this batch does not pretend that they do.

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 41–49

A later writer must not change these without recording the change. Several are load-bearing for Volume 02.

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| The reed gauge, first figure ever written | **three-sixteenths of an inch above the mark, pump on the idle**, at the fourth hour of the eleventh of the month; and the **mark itself measured for the first time at nine and a quarter inches off the sill of the main**. The clamp went on the twenty-first of last month, so the gauge is **twenty days old** at the first reading and not twenty-one. No earlier reading exists and the entry of the twenty-first of last month says the reading cannot be supplied. **A number with no baseline and no load is not evidence and not a claim** | Ch 41 |
| Why the full-draw load is not applied to the seal | **the load test was the twentieth day's act, on a certificate, with three names on it, and there is no charter to hang a second one on.** It is **not** her absence — a licensed engineer is in the fen from the eleventh onward. Ms Quill says she will not spend a second act on that head in that condition | Ch 41, and it survives to the end of the batch |
| The schedule of damage, the margin note | **amount not disputed. Sum not received. No facility exists in the form for non-payment and none is provided.** Entered in the margin of the applicant of record at the request of the clerk on the **forty-second** | Ch 42 |
| Why the form has nowhere to record non-payment | **the *amount disputed* box was struck out by Vane's advice on the fourth, so the form carries the amount and no box for whether it has been paid** | Ch 42 |
| The **£14 10** for the second reader, and who paid it | **the Salt March Dryfoot Trust, out of the annex, in Tova Reed's own hand, against the trust, received on the fifteenth.** Two refusals, both correct (Anneke Roos on a creditor in a document; Marek on the third exchange requirement), overridden by Tova Reed on her own terms. **It is not a loan, a gift, or a rescue, and the March's first row of its own line is thinner by that sum** | Ch 42, Ch 44 |
| The weep, on the fourteenth | **half an inch and a sixteenth, twice measured, two rules, at the seventh hour of the fourteenth, in daylight and not at the fourth hour in the dark, pump on and continuous for nineteen hours, and this is not a weep, it is a leak.** Water is passing into the housing and the pit is not rising, and the pit **has four feet of iron water in it and is not up a quarter of an inch** — it is not dry. **Cause not established.** Unchanged at the seventh hour of the fifteenth, when the pump is on and continuous for **fifty-five hours**, which is nineteen plus the thirty-six hours between the two readings. **The load the fen applies is not the load the log states and nobody applied anything** | Ch 43, Ch 44 |
| The beat, eighth logging | **nine in the quarter hour, four of them together at the end, on the eleventh of the month, licensed person present *none*, cause not established, and nothing written underneath it** | Ch 41 |
| The second levy | **sixteen households, fourteen signatures, the same fourteen, the two who did not sign last month did not sign this month, the two who put nothing in last time put nothing in this time, the woman with the shilling put the shilling in again and wrote the same three words under it, and the two who signed only so as not to be the ones who had not signed again signed first. Sum: four pounds five**, the same sum as the levy of the twelfth, out of the same sixteen houses. **The two levies are not two deliveries: the first is in the box and the second went to compost on the fourteenth** | Ch 42, Ch 43 |
| The compost put in on the fourteenth, not the first | **twenty-one hundredweight and a quarter, at four shillings, weighed at the gate, and the first time the second wording has been used on a day that was not the first of a month.** That is **about a twentieth** of a month's specification and not a fifth; the fifth was a drafting error and is corrected in Ch 49 and in the state files. A hundredweight is worth about half a degree on the bed, so this is about a tenth of a degree, **and a soil iron cannot read a tenth of a degree, so the figure Tova Reed enters is the hundredweight and not the degree** | Ch 43, Ch 47 |
| The bed, at the end of the batch | **three and a half degrees**, measured twice with a soil iron on the first, the fourteenth and the twentieth. Not three and three quarters. The higher figure was drafted and cut as un-Arithmetic | Ch 39, 43, 47, 49 |
| The four records, at the water's edge, on the seventeenth | **one, the cooperative's own log, six seasons, never audited. Two, Marek's own gauge — a length of cheap reed let into a strip of tin with a brass mark scratched into the tin, clamped at the eleventh rod on the twenty-first of last month, whose reed went under at the fourth hour of the night of the sixteenth with the return main under eleven inches of fen water and which therefore has no second figure and proves nothing except that a man built one for four shillings. Three, the miller's chalk, four hundred and nineteen, brought up the fen road on a slate at the eleventh hour by a boy of fifteen and copied into the cooperative's own book. Four, the dated seed-house ledger.** Kellan Rusk's own log arrives at the eleventh hour and is put on the bench as a fifth thing, and **Sera Quill signs under the four** | Ch 46 |
| **The eighty a day** | the log claims the head gives **eleven hundred**; the ground takes **seven hundred and twenty**; the difference is **three hundred and eighty**; **Kellan's own book says his side draw has never been above three hundred.** **Eighty turns a day unaccounted for, and eighty a day for six years is a hundred and seventy-five thousand two hundred turns.** Said out loud on an embankment, in front of a district inspector, by the engineer of record, on the seventeenth, and entered in the cooperative's own book as the only entry of the season that is against both parties | Ch 46 |
| The return main as built against specification | **twenty-two rods of eight inch against forty-one rods of twelve — rather more than a third. The miller's board is nine miles off in Rowan Teale's own yard at Fennmere and has been for thirty-one years; the highest figure on it was two hundred and sixty-one, in the spring of the year Aldren went** | Ch 46 |
| The wheel on the crest night | **four hundred and nineteen**, carried up the fen road at the eleventh hour of the night of the sixteenth by a mill boy of fifteen on a road that was under at the second gate, **higher than anything on that board in thirty-one years**, against a main that can pass about **three hundred and sixty**. **The extra sixty came up by something that is not the return main, and nobody in that yard knows what** | Ch 45, Ch 46 |
| The night's work, the sixteenth into the seventeenth | **the nine-foot toe ditch taken to fourteen feet wide and four foot six deep with the lip off the inside of the toe, and a hundred and forty yards of old channel and a culvert rodded out with a hook and a bar by five people between the ninth hour and the fourth hour.** Five people: Marek Vale and Ilsa Kern on the corner, Jonas Byre and two levy men on the channel, and **Jonas Byre went into nothing at all and counted the minutes out loud and wrote down whatever the hook brought out of the culvert and how big.** The channel runs again at about the second hour and the well house stays dry. **There is no new run anywhere in it** | Ch 45, Ch 46 |
| **The fieldbook's own three lines, on the seventeenth** | **the volume's last panel, and the instrument's and not Sera Quill's.** *Cut the upper and leave the lower. Wait a season, and the ground will come back. Cut again, and put your name to it.* Read at the copper seam by Sera Quill, eleven seconds, contact tested with a plain wire gauge and the reading written down before she went on. **They are not the three lines of the twenty-fourth of last month and the record says so before the lines are.** **No document quotes them as a reason or as an instruction, and the hearing officer has never seen them** | Ch 46 |
| **Sera Quill's finding, on the seventeenth, which is not a panel** | *This was set, and not remembered. It is a pattern to keep to a season that has not come. And the keeping of it is the work, and nobody is coming.* Entered by her, in her hand, under her name, with the hour. **The finding is hers and not the instrument's, and the state files may not call it a panel** | Ch 46 |
| **The use log, on the morning of the seventeenth** | **four lines.** The first is the tenth of last month, entered on the eleventh, and its lateness was declared in an officer's room on the eighth. The second is the twentieth of last month, the certificate and the seal. The third is the fourth of this month, a refusal to open it, with the reason. **The fourth is the seventeenth at four in the morning, on the back of the page, initialled by Lissa Vale herself, and it is a line that says a thing came out of a press.** The whole of it is read aloud at the bench and again in the room on the twenty-first, in nine minutes both times. **This row is the count as it stood on the seventeenth and it is not superseded: the log had four lines then, five from Chapter 57 and six from Chapter 63, and a later writer must use six. See *The use log, six lines, from Chapter 63* in the Batch 0002 section** | Ch 46, Ch 48 |
| **Lissa Vale's undertaking, broken and entered** | **she undertook in writing on the eighth to produce the instrument on the twenty-first and not before, on a form, in an appointed officer's room, with the use log entire.** She produced it on the seventeenth, four days early, at a bench, in a flood, and **declared the breach herself, in one answer, and entered it as the lateness in the same words she entered the lateness of the first line of the use log.** The reason she gave is her own and is in the record: the water was over the fourth yard of that yard at four in the morning, the press is on the inside wall of the seed house, she is the woman who keeps it, and she sat up with it for two hours. **The March's tin never left the shelf and the seed house's undertaking that it will not be moved held** | Ch 46 |
| **Idris Vane's broken condition, and the envelope** | **his first condition was not to be told what the first reader read, with the first reader's record sealed in an envelope under Lissa Vale's name, not to be opened before he has read the node and not at all if it is broken early. Marek told him on the fourth, at the eleventh hour, in his own kitchen, and Vane wrote it out in front of him.** Vane raised it himself on the fifteenth, declared the condition broken, and **the envelope is now not to be opened at all, by anybody, ever** — and he wrote to Lissa Vale to say so. **It is reported in his own attested copy of the eighteenth, with the date of the telling, and the officer minuted it on the twenty-first. The applicant's copy was not shown to him first, and his figures went to the district first** | Ch 44, Ch 46, Ch 48 |
| Renn Tallow's docket | **one sheet, torn from a nail in a yard at Fennmere on the eighteenth day of last month. Item N-2214/K. Tools drawn against the item: chain, block, dies, grafting set. Order date: not on the docket. Work description: none.** **This is not the works schedule and the schedule is still never produced** | Ch 46 |
| The name in the box | **an office and not a man. Schedule clerk, fen drains.** The post has existed longer than Kellan Rusk; the man in it has changed four times that he knows of; **he does not come out. He signs the paper. The paper goes to a store. The store gives out the kit.** Sera Quill heard it and said nothing, and nobody asked her a fourth time. **The reader does not yet understand what it means and the volume does not say** | Ch 46 |
| Renn Tallow's gate statement | **asked to be recorded at the weir on the night of the tenth day of last month — that the branch below Weir Row has had nothing coming up it since the spring before last, that every man in the low quarter knows it, and that a statement of it was asked to be entered in the book of the body responsible. Nobody recorded it.** Entered on the nineteenth of this month, **the lateness is thirty-nine days**, in the seed house book and not in the certified standing log | Ch 47 |
| The low quarter's own map | **four men of the low quarter chalked the line of the standing iron on every gatepost, door step and cellar wall at the height of the iron, on the nineteenth and the twentieth, at their own hand and their own cost, on a stick and a bucket of lime.** Not the cooperative's work, not the cooperative's suggestion. **Marek did not go and said so in the book** | Ch 47 |
| Nia Vale | **not in this batch and not in the volume.** She left on the twentieth day of last month on a hired cart with a seed consignment and a sealed letter in it, and she does not come back. **The letter came into the seed house on the nineteenth of this month, by hand, sealed, unopened, brought by a person who went on up the road and did not stop, and nobody in the fen opened it or wrote down why it was not delivered.** Anneke Roos entered the fact that this body wrote to its contractor and did not send the letter, in her own hand, with the hour, and did not enter the reason because she was not given it. Marek entered one sentence about it and the sentence was about himself: he has not asked where it went or how long it was in a cart, and he is not going to. **The letter is in the open drawer of the seed house table with the levy papers and the rent book** | Ch 47, Ch 49 |
| The collie at the gateway, on the nineteenth | **an old black-and-white collie with the left ear off came up the fen road while Renn Tallow's statement was being entered, stood in the gateway, would not come in, and went back up the road at about the eleventh hour with its tail down. Joss Pyle's grandmother's dog is nine miles off in the low quarter and this one has no collar and no name in this yard. Gael Senn looked at it from the step for about a minute, wrote in the cooperative's own book that she had decided to write nothing about the animal, with her name on the decision and not the animal's, and read it back before anybody else had read it. Nobody went out to it, and nobody asked her about it.** The state files previously dated this beat to the seventeenth, which is Ch 46 and is not where it happens | Ch 47 |
| The conditional charter | **four clauses and a fifth sheet, made at two o'clock on the twenty-first, for the season, expiring at the end of the season without notice.** Node stays open; head not sealed, seed stock not taken, not referred for relocation. Standing pressure log and monthly root-change report **adopted by the district as conditions at the applicant's request, and the district did not invent them**. Return main to specification by a date to be fixed and fed at not less than the specified hundredweight a month, with the three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight shortfall **carried forward and not discharged**. The seed annex of the ninth is a condition, the Dryfoot Trust is a party, and the fourth term (a copy of everything to Cray whether or not it helps) is a condition | Ch 48 |
| The fifth sheet | **the monthly examination and the return line, including the second wording: where a thing required by the charter did not happen, the entry for that line records that it did not happen, in the same book, on the same page, in the same hand, with the hour.** Argued for nine minutes and won. **The first examination under it is the first of next month, ten days after the charter, and it may be under a foot of fen** | Ch 48 |
| The schedule of damage at the sitting | **not discharged, not remitted, not reduced.** The officer has no power over it; a debt is enforceable against a body; **a body is a thing with a charter and this body had none from the eighteenth of last month, and there is nobody in the world you can serve sixty pounds ten shillings on.** The sum stands in the file, the file stays open, the emergency clause stays live. **The Directorate reserves the right to reopen this file on any new report** | Ch 48 |
| Kellan's monitored charter | **the private charter survives.** A gauge fixed **below the split** of a pattern any party may read, the reading entered in a book kept in Fennmere and not in Fennmere only; his log open to any member of the cooperative at any hour without notice; a return to the district every month with a copy to the seed house; a person may be sent to his pump house at any time by a hearing officer on a day's notice with the reason minuted; no variation of the draw without a form. **The Lantern Reach contract is gone and he does not appeal it** | Ch 48 |
| Hester Carrow's authority | **her authority over charters runs to the end of the year**, which is the same year Inspector Bray is charter officer, and she says she has never once seen a year used as a reason to do less | Ch 48 |
| The compost return, permanently | **bought, at four shillings, about a twentieth of what the graft was designed to be fed, from sixteen households who can raise four pounds five in a fortnight, twice.** Marek's own note, entered against the interest of the application: nobody will be able to see the starve from the top of the field, and it will be visible in the second week of next August, and by then it will be somebody else's log | Ch 49 |
| The first row of next season's line | **the Longneck, eleven trays' worth, out of the trust's own material, sown on the twenty-second of the month in a wet drill, up in four hours, leaning eleven degrees off the vertical along its whole length.** **The word *failed* is not written anywhere, by Gael Senn's decision, and the eleven degrees go in the book and in the copy** | Ch 49 |
| The section-nine margin notes | **six, in the same book on the same pages, all closing with *this is worth nothing against a cut*, and the sixth is the only one of the six that is not about a cut** | Ch 45, Ch 49 |

## New locks from the drafted Batch 0005

- **The bypass is unbuilt and the volume is over.** It is refused twice on five correct grounds, offered free and refused, and the four men are turned round at the gate on the eleventh by their own man, **and nobody digs it for Lantern Reach.** The climax pays the *idea* by an improvised cut of the cooperative's own making, **and what the cooperative actually dug is not a new run at all**: the nine-foot toe ditch made bigger — fourteen feet wide, four foot six deep, the lip off the inside of the toe — and a hundred and forty yards of old channel and a culvert rodded out. **Marek says out loud, in a room, on the sixteenth of a month, that a bigger hole in a bank he has already cut is the same hand on the same bank in the same hour, that a man in a room in four years will be right about it, and that it will be the whole of what he finds, because there is no new run in it and there is not going to be one.** A later writer may not have anyone say the bypass was built, and **may not have a new run of thirty or forty yards at the head side into the bed below Weir Row appear in any later chapter.** The drafted version of this batch had exactly that cut, and it was removed: see *The Batch 0005 review pass*, finding 2.
- **The instrument is never opened by an unlicensed hand and is not opened in an officer's room.** Lissa Vale takes it out of the press at four in the morning of the seventeenth, in the dark, **leaves the March's tin on the shelf where it was**, writes the hour on the back of the use log and initials it herself, **and declares on the bench that she has broken her own written undertaking to produce it on the twenty-first and not before, entering the breach as the lateness in the same words she entered the lateness of the first line of the use log, and giving the reason in her own hand because the reason is hers.** Ansel Bray objects **as an officer** and is not able to put a rule behind it and says so; **nobody in her room may open it, including her, and the ruling binds her successor**; the instrument is still produced on a form on the twenty-first and **the officer does not open it either**. It sits on the table in the seed house from the twenty-second. **The reading at the water's edge was the use log read aloud, a licensed engineer's own three lines from the instrument, and a licensed engineer's finding — the instrument was opened by Sera Quill with her own clip, at a bench, and not by a man and not in a room.**
- **The clean record is still gone and the instrument is still not back in the press.** From the eighth of the month the fieldbook is a district file that cannot be recalled. On the twenty-second Lissa puts the instrument and the use log **on the middle of the seed-house table** where anybody may look and anybody may ask to have the log read to them, and it is **not** in the press, and this is her fourth refusal of the season, unnumbered and not explained. **A later writer may not put it back in the press and may not make her sorry and may not make her thanked.**
- **The use log's first line is in a district record and the lateness is in the record, and neither is re-discovered.** Both are read out at the water's edge and both are read out again in the room on the twenty-first, and the officer records that the applicant taught the office its own file on the eighth.
- **The weep is a leak and is not resolved into anything.** Half an inch and a sixteenth, measured twice, at the six o'clock corner, under the fen's own head and not under the load stated, and the pit does not rise because the housing is doing the only job it has. **It is still a leak at the end of the volume and it is not mended, and nobody in the batch mends it, because there is no contractor, no license and no tar and the only way to take the load off is to stop the pump.**
- **The monthly examination on the first of every month is a charter condition and the next one is day 61, ten days after the charter, and it may be under a foot of fen.** Gael Senn has said she will be there for it and has written nothing in advance, in the room, in front of the officer, and the officer minuted that. **Nobody may fill that log for her.**
- **The pressure pulse is logged eight times and is still undiagnosed.** The eighth is nine in the quarter hour with four together at the end, no licensed person present, *cause not established*, and **nothing is written underneath it, by Gael Senn's decision and her name on it.** Nobody in Volume 01 may name or explain it.
- **The third exchange requirement is still missing on the one licensed work in the volume and it is now a charter condition.** The new run is designed to be fed four hundred hundredweight a month and is going to be fed about a twentieth of that, and the shortfall of three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight a month is carried forward on the charter and not discharged. **A later chapter may not quietly fill it.**
- **Dunmar's *none known* box is still spent and was not spent again.** The clock being twenty days old and the box being a good reason to be careful is a thing the chapter says out loud, and Dunmar does not open it, and **the entry of the twenty-eighth's form, in which he said he had priced it twice and not spent it, is the largest thing in that chapter and the chapter does not make him use it.**
- **The cooperative's own books are still not a filing and are not an accepted correction.** They are now a charter condition, which is a different and larger thing, and **the Crown adopted them at the applicant's request and did not invent them, and that is why they become revisable.** The motion to enter the claim in the log was still lost nine to twenty-two on the fourth of last month and there is still no corrected draw figure in the district's hands.
- **The Thornwild refusal stands exactly as the record has it.** The burn **lapsed on the twelfth by its own terms because it rained**, and the cooperative neither consented nor refused, and **no party is to be treated as having decided anything.** Nobody in this batch names the settlement, uses the long line, quotes it, or asks that it be named, and the word *seat* does not appear.
- **The pathhound report is in three places and is still not interpreted.** The collie comes up the fen road on the **nineteenth** and stands in the gateway and will not come in, and goes back up the road at about the eleventh hour, and **Gael Senn decides not to write anything about it and enters the decision with her name on it and not the animal's.** The low quarter then walks and chalks its own line, on its own hand, on the nineteenth and the twentieth, and **Marek does not go**, and says in the book that nobody is going to walk it and that nothing is ever going to be written underneath the sixth of the month's report. **Nobody may interpret the animal, nobody may use it as a symbol, and the weir animal does not return.**
- **The crew split is healed on the page and only on the page.** Ilsa Kern says she was wrong about the west side of the windbreak and Jonas Byre says he will hold the top end and count the minutes out loud. **Nobody apologizes, nobody is forgiven, and nobody is softened by the flood.** The burn did not happen and nobody signed anything.
- **The seed annex is signed and is now a condition of a charter, and the trust's name is on the record against the trust's own interest six times.** The March's tin is **still in the press, thirty-three days at risk on the twenty-second**, and the annex still says the seed house does not undertake to move it and the trust does not ask it to, and **nobody moved it.**
- **Nia does not appear in this batch or in this volume.** She left on the twentieth of last month and she is not brought back, and the lock in the batch prompt is honored: *if it is delivered, deliver it by hand, on the page, in a chapter.* **The letter comes back into the yard sealed, by hand, on the nineteenth, unopened, and nobody writes down why it was not delivered because the person who carried it did not give the reason and nobody went after her.** The cost of the non-delivery is entered by Anneke Roos in her own hand and the fact of it is entered by Marek in his own hand, and **both of those entries are about this body and neither is about her.** Whether she ever hands it over belongs to nobody but her.
- **Renn Tallow's unrecorded refusal is now recorded and the lateness is declared.** Thirty-nine days, in the seed house book, because the certified standing log cannot be added to without a district officer in the room. **Nobody apologizes to him and he does not apologize.**
- **Idris Vane is paid, on the fifteenth, and reads on the eighteenth, and is not a rescue and not a favor.** He refuses to take a creditor's money, refuses a condition that would let the reading be held back, says what the reading is worth before he has read anything, and **the applicant's copy was not shown to him first**, and his figures went to the district first and the cooperative second. **He raises his own broken condition himself — *not to be told what the first reader read* — in his own kitchen on the fifteenth, eleven days after this body broke it, and puts it in his own attested copy on the eighteenth, and the officer minutes it on the twenty-first. The sealed envelope it broke is never to be opened by anybody, at any time, and is not.** His own fieldbook was not on the table and was not asked about. **He is sixty-one and does not appear again in Volume 01.** The draft state line saying he is sixty-eight in Volume 02 was wrong and is corrected here.
- **The fieldbook panel is spent and the volume's allowance is now zero, and the panel is the instrument's three lines and not Sera Quill's finding.** The one unspent panel was used once, at the copper seam, on the bench, on the seventeenth, and it reads: *Cut the upper and leave the lower. Wait a season, and the ground will come back. Cut again, and put your name to it.* **Sera Quill's own finding, entered by her in her hand under her own name, is a separate three-line block — *This was set, and not remembered. It is a pattern to keep to a season that has not come. And the keeping of it is the work, and nobody is coming* — and it is a licensed finding and not a panel, and no state file may call it one.** **No document in this batch quotes the three lines as a reason or as an instruction, and the officer has never seen them.**
- **Sera Quill says the deeper answer plainly in Chapter 46 and stops there.** What is in the bead and in the cut face is a **work order**: a shape set down in advance, in the order a man works in when he means to bring a thing about. It is a **target pattern**, and the word for a target pattern is not a prophecy, it is a specification. She has seen the structure of one before, has not wanted to say so for twenty-three years, and **will not say where she saw it, what wrote it, or why a pattern of that shape would be put into living wood at all.** Those three are put in the record as questions and answered at a hearing, not on a bench. **Nobody names who, what, or why. Her fourth refusal is unnumbered, unopened and unexplained, and Lissa Vale's refusal of the same number is a different thing and is not confused with it.**
- **Marek is Stage 1 at the end of the volume, and the stage is unreliable, costly, and dependent on a licensed witness.** He gets four seconds from a cut face at Weir Row on the seventeenth, it is dearer than four seconds, it costs him the ground under his feet, the third contact of the season, and **he is unverified and cannot be corroborated by anybody**, and Gael Senn writes in her own book that what she heard on the bench was a licensed engineer's three lines and not his account, and that his account of the fourth hour is a rumor with a witness in it, which is her phrase, used about him on purpose. **He can feel a pressure in wood with his hand on it. He cannot draw, cannot graft, has no bead, and has no rootmark — there is nothing on his arm and he has looked at it twice since the seventeenth.** The first skin rootmark is Volume 02.
- **Marek keeps no instrument, no private log and no private advantage.** The instrument is on his mother's table, the log is a book anybody may read, and the copy is in a file that cannot be recalled. He has a certificate he cannot use without a licensed witness, a monthly obligation he argued against for nine minutes, and a fen that knows what he did in the dark on the night of the tenth of last month and has never mentioned it to anybody.
- **Nothing romantic happened and nothing was softened.** Tova Reed overrode two correct refusals to pay for the reading, and **the override is the refusal's cost written on a document against nine thousand people who are not in the room**, and that is what happened. She is not a rescue and Marek is not saved. Lissa Vale is not forgiven and does not say anything about it.

## Volume 01 closed — the facts, in one place

1. **The charter.** A **conditional charter**, four clauses and a fifth sheet, made at two o'clock on the twenty-first of the month, **for the season, expiring at the end of the season without notice and without anything being said.**
2. **The node stays open.** The head is not sealed, the seed stock is not taken, and the charter is not referred for relocation.
3. **The cooperative's own obligations are now charter conditions**: the standing pressure log, open to any member, any stranger and any clerk without notice, and the monthly report of root changes. **Adopted by the district at the applicant's request. The district did not invent them**, which is the point, and it is also how they become revisable — the district may not amend them this season without the consent of the body.
4. **The return main** is to be made to its specification of forty-one rods of twelve inch by a date to be fixed, and fed at not less than the specified hundredweight a month, and **the shortfall of three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight a month is carried forward and not discharged.**
5. **The seed annex of the ninth is a condition**, the Salt March Dryfoot Trust is a named party, its material may not be used, moved, sold or removed without the steward's word, and its fourth term — a copy of everything to Cray whether or not it helps — is a condition of the charter.
6. **The fifth sheet** is the monthly examination and the return line, **including the second wording**, which is now in a district charter for the first time, and the first examination under it is on the first of next month, ten days after the charter, and may be under a foot of fen.
7. **The schedule of damage of sixty pounds ten is not discharged, not remitted and not reduced.** It stands in the file. A debt is enforceable against a body and a body is a thing with a charter, so the sum is entered and not owed to anybody, and the district's only remedy is the emergency clause, on a report, at a time of its choosing. **The emergency clause stays live.**
8. **What lapses:** the charter, at the end of the season. **The instrument's production undertaking, discharged on the twenty-first.** The district's controlled burn, lapsed on the twelfth by its own terms.
9. **What the Directorate reserves:** the right to reopen the file on any new report, stated in the minute, and not tradeable. **The file stays open.** That reservation is the handoff to Volume 02.
10. **Kellan's falsified log is a matter of record.** The Lantern Reach contract is gone and is not appealed. **His private charter survives under monitored conditions** — a gauge below the split that any party may read, an open log, a monthly return, a person may be sent — **because Fennmere's low quarter of forty-one houses still drinks off the bed that his branch feeds and there is no other water in that quarter for nine miles.** He is not killed, not fled, not redeemed, and he testified for an hour and ten minutes and gave it badly.
11. **The compost return is rebuilt permanently with bought compost and a thinner heap**, at four shillings, about a twentieth of what the graft was designed to be fed, from sixteen households who can raise four pounds five in a fortnight twice. **The graft that made the field green is never fed the way it was designed to be fed**, and nobody in this building ever said the graft was working.
12. **The dry-land line is still lost.** Three trays went into a coupling on the third day of last month, the last of the March's material on this side of the water is a tin in a press, at risk on the thirty-third day and unmoved, and the March's first row of its own line is thinner by fourteen pounds ten.
13. **The three acres are gone.** Three seasons of Marek's own ground, worked separately from the shared field, was under the water for a week and comes up sour, and it will not crop until the third season after this one, and **it is not in anybody's book as a loss to anybody but him, and it is in the book as a decision.**
14. **The nineteen acres stand and the seed house stands**, and the first row of next season's line is in the ground and leaning eleven degrees off the vertical, because that is what the land is doing this year.
15. **The pathhound line is mapped and left alone.** The low quarter walked and chalked it itself, on its own hand and at its own cost, and nobody in Tarrow Fen went, and nothing was ever written underneath the report.
16. **Marek's answer to the volume's question is a license and an obligation, not a possession.** He is the engineer of record on a charter that expires in a season, on a node he may not keep, under a return that is a twentieth of what it needs, holding a certificate he cannot use without somebody standing next to him. **The seed stock is not seized and the first harvest comes in short and real.**

## Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0005

- **Three contacts, not two, and the third is the scarroot of a cut face at Weir Row on the morning of the seventeenth.** It is a contact with a dead-and-scarring piece of cut wood, it is not a fieldbook, and it is four seconds. It cost him the ground under his feet, an hour he cannot account for, and the standing of his own account. It is **unverifiable and uncorroborated and is worth nothing in front of anybody except a licensed engineer**, which is the trade.
- **Stage 1, Touchroot, at the end of the volume.** Unreliable, costly, and dependent on a licensed witness. **No rootmark. Nothing on his arm. No bead, no anchor work, no bridge above local conduit, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no reading of the deep archive.** **The first skin rootmark is Volume 02 and belongs to his first independent graft.**
- **Sera Quill checked his arm four times in the season and not a fifth.** She looked at him on the seventeenth with a cloth and did not ask what he got, and said it was the correct state of affairs and would be for the rest of his life.
- **No Fieldbook panel remains in the volume, and there are four of them, not two.** Each batch prompt allowed one panel in its batch, and **four batches spent it: Ch 6 (Batch 0001, at the moment of choice about the head), Ch 19 (Batch 0002, the load choice), Ch 24 (Batch 0003, the choice about the new run) and Ch 46 (Batch 0005, the bench at the copper seam). Batch 0004 spent none and said so.** The Ch 46 one is the instrument's own three lines, and Sera Quill's finding of the same morning is **not** a panel. **The Batch 0001 stage accounting above never recorded the Ch 6 panel, which is why this line had said two; the panel was sanctioned by that batch's own prompt and it is in the prose.** No allowance remains for Volume 01 and the allowance is now zero.
- **Sera Quill's name is on four documents in Fennmere and none of them is one she was thanked for.** Her certification stands with her personal liability on it, she went down the road on the afternoon cart on the twenty-second with her fee on an invoice and eight pages of notes, and **she has not been told by anybody in this fen that she was right, and nobody is going to.**
- **Marda Vole and the Undercommons do not appear.** Ivo Tarrant appears once, on the eleventh, and turns the four men round, and the appearance is a refusal. **The four men are not a rescue, they do not dig anything, and they are not coming back.**

## Spelling, decided and recorded

`bible/characters.md`, `outline/` and the Volume 01 plan locks say US spelling. **The prose did not follow them and the manuscript was mixed** — about 111 British instances against about 75 American ones, with *fiber* and *color* mostly American, the noun split across two spellings against its own verb, and every other word in the set mostly British.

**Decided once, at the Volume 01 close: US spelling governs.** 111 instances were converted as a single mechanical pass over all forty-nine chapters: the noun *license*, which had been spelled two ways against its own verb across twenty-seven chapters, and *rumor*, *gray*, *labor*, *favor*, *color*, *program*, *defense*, *neighbor*, *honor*, *traveling*, *canceled* and *judgment*. **The bible's own seven slips were corrected**, and the state and review records were brought into line (85 instances). The decision is written into `bible/terminology.md` so the bible and the prose cannot contradict each other silently. *Toward* and *towards* are both standard in US English, and the two instances in the prose were made *toward* rather than left as a variant.

**A later writer must not reintroduce British forms into the prose, the bible, the outlines or the state files.**

## Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Batch 0005

Batch 0005 added seven small things by drafting. All are local detail, mechanisms, places, or people, and none changes a world rule:

1. **The district works docket** — a one-sheet record of a tool kit drawn out of a district store against an item number, with no order date and no work description on it. It is a document a foreman can take off a nail in a yard, and it is **not** the works schedule and does not produce one.
2. **The office of *schedule clerk, fen drains***, and the fact that a district works order is signed by a post and not by a man, and that the man in the post does not come out. This is the shape of the Crown's paperwork as a working body experiences it, and it is deliberately a shape and not a villain.
3. **Perrin Osse's margin entry of the forty-second** — *amount not disputed, sum not received, no facility exists in the form for non-payment* — and the resulting structural fact that **a schedule of damage against a body with no charter is a sum that is entered and not owed, and the district's only remedy is the emergency clause.** This is the volume's political last move and it is a procedure, not a person.
4. **The conditional charter's five sheets**, including the fifth, which adopts Gael Senn's second wording into district charter law. A new instrument class: a charter that expires at the end of a season by its own terms.
5. **Kellan's monitored charter and its six conditions**, written by a district clerk who said in advance that she was not the district.
6. **The nine-foot toe ditch at the low corner**, made bigger in the night to fourteen feet and four foot six, and **a hundred and forty yards of old channel and a culvert rodded out with a hook and a bar**, both of them the cooperative's own hand and its own existing works, the ditch entered as a section-nine margin note, and **Marek's own sentence that a bigger hole in a bank he has already cut is the same hand on the same bank in the same hour and will be the whole of what a man in a room finds.** The bypass itself remains unbuilt and the four men remain unasked. **A seventh item was drafted and withdrawn in the review pass:** the thirty-or-forty-yard new run to Weir Row, which was the bypass by every test the volume had set, was removed from Chapters 45 and 46 and is not canon. **A small physical fact is also canon and matters downstream: the culvert under the fen road at Weir Row was shut with a season of silt, a fortnight of rain and a drowned alder, and Jonas Byre wrote down what the hook brought out of it and how big.**
7. **The low quarter's own lime-and-stick survey** of the line of the standing iron, done by its own people at its own cost, and the fact that it is on a piece of paper in Fennmere and is the only survey of that ground anybody has ever had.


---

## The Batch 0005 review pass — by hand, and what it changed

**The review phase's standing failure to load the `novel-reviewer` subagent meant Batch 0005 was never reviewed by the pipeline. The log of the attempt is `logs/batch-0005.review.log`; the findings, as reported, are in `reviews/batch-0005.findings.md`; they were applied to the nine chapters in place, without restarting the batch and without changing the planned plot.** Nothing below is a change to the plan. Every item is a repair of the prose or of a state file that did not match the prose.

### The nine blocking findings, and what each one cost

1. **Nia came back.** `chapter-0047.md` had her return on the nineteenth with the letter, against hard lock 15 and three statements in the outline. **Her return is cut.** The letter now comes into the yard **sealed, by hand, on the nineteenth**, unopened, brought by somebody who went on up the road, and nobody in the fen writes down why it was not delivered. The cost of the non-delivery is entered by Anneke Roos in her own hand and the fact of it is entered by Marek in his own hand, and both entries are about this body rather than about her. **She does not appear again and is not in the volume.**
2. **The bypass got dug.** The drafted Chapters 45–46 had a thirty-or-forty-yard new run dug by hand from the top of the field to the old channel at Weir Row, which is the outline's own definition of the refused bypass, and Chapter 45 had Marek say so in a lawyer's sentence. **That new run is removed entirely.** The night's work is now the **same hole made bigger and the old channel rodded out**, both existing works of this holding, both entered and both bounded in the book. Marek keeps the honest beat and loses the false one: a bigger hole in a bank he has already cut is the same hand on the same bank in the same hour, and a man in a room in four years will be right about it and it will be the whole of what he finds. **The bypass is unbuilt, unfunded, and may not appear in any later chapter.**
3. **Lissa Vale broke her own written undertaking and never said so.** She produced the instrument four days early, Bray raised it, and her answer addressed *opening* rather than *producing*. **She now declares the breach herself, in one answer, on the bench, and enters it as the lateness in the same words she entered the lateness of the first line of the use log**, and gives the reason in her own hand because the reason is hers: the water was over the fourth yard of that yard at four in the morning and the press is on the inside wall of the seed house and she is the woman who keeps it.
4. **The March's tin was moved out of the press.** Chapter 46 had it out from under the ledger with the instrument. **It now never leaves the shelf**, and says why: a tin of seed is not a document, and she was not going to move a thing in a press in the dark because her hands were full. **Thirty-three days at risk, unmoved, as Ch 39 and the state files always said.**
5. **The use log had four entries and was called two lines.** **It is four lines**, and they are now named: the tenth of last month entered on the eleventh, the twentieth of last month, the refusal of the fourth of this month, and the seventeenth at four in the morning on the back of the page in her own hand.
6. **Chapter 47 dated the clerk's visit and the sitting to a Tuesday.** The clerk came on the **twentieth**, a Sunday, and the sitting is on the **twenty-first**, a Monday. **There is no Tuesday in that sequence any more**, including in Anneke Roos's line about where the fact is going to be said.
7. **Chapter 46 contradicted itself on the mill board.** Two hundred and sixty-one and four hundred and nineteen could not both be the highest figure in thirty-one years. **Two hundred and sixty-one is now the highest the board had; four hundred and nineteen is higher than anything on it in thirty-one years.**
8. **Chapter 43 timed one examination two ways and Chapter 44's pump arithmetic did not run.** The examination is at the **seventh hour of the fourteenth, in daylight**, matching the log entry and the licensed reader's own words; and the pump, on for nineteen hours at that reading, is on for **fifty-five hours** at the seventh hour of the fifteenth. The sentence that said *the pit is dry* while four feet of iron-colored water stood in it now says the pit **has four feet of water in it and is not up a quarter of an inch**, which is what the log entry always said.
9. **Two state files recorded a scene that was not in the batch.** The collie at the gateway was claimed for the seventeenth, which is Chapter 46 and not where it happens. **The beat was written into Chapter 47 on the nineteenth**, where the state files said it was and where the state of the yard allows it, and the state files were corrected. Nothing else in the two files had to change to match, and the claim is now true of the prose.

### The should-fix items, all applied

The six-word choice is now made **once**; the act with no words at the end of Chapter 44 was cut and replaced with the rule that a book cannot hold a sentence before the man who owns it has said it in the yard where it will happen. **Sera Quill now takes the examination in daylight in a yard**, which is what she said on the eleventh she would do, and the reversal costs a clause instead of a paragraph. **The miller's board is nine miles off in his own yard at Fennmere** and is not across from the seed house. **Marek's own gauge is one of the four records on the bench**, with the reason it proves nothing, and the dated seed-house ledger is named as the fourth; **Kellan's own log arrives at the eleventh hour as the fifth thing and is where the eighty a day comes from.** **Idris Vane is in the resolution**: his attested reading of the eighteenth is the fifth thing on the file, the officer reads both documents side by side and declines to decide between two licensed readers, and the register carries his own disclosure that he was told what the first reader read before he read the node — **which is a condition of his own notice, broken by this body on the fourth, reported by him on the fifteenth, and minuted on the twenty-first.** The compost fraction is **a twentieth** in Chapter 49 and in every state file. **The two levies now have compatible rosters** — the two households that put nothing in last time put nothing in this time, and the shilling and the three words under it are the same hand twice — and the two sums are not two deliveries. The trust vote **has a division**, five to three, with the two who had refused not voting. The eight people in the seed house on the twelfth are **eight people in the seed house on the twelfth**. **The sitting's own order now fits the morning it describes**, the officer says she wrote her order at four in the morning and will not pretend to know how the day will run, and the charter at about two o'clock is the end of a morning that began at half past nine. **The fieldbook's three lines are now a panel** — a three-line block, the house style of Chapters 6 and 19 — **and Sera Quill's finding is a separate three-line block in her own hand, and the state files no longer call the finding a panel.** Four garbled sentences are repaired: *the thirteenth is the thirteenth day*; *one day, not a day, on the fourteenth day*; *selling you the second thing, which is what the second thing is for*; and the register line about Nia and eleven miles that the cut has now removed with it.

### The minor items, all applied or recorded

*Girl* of twenty-three is *woman*, in the manuscript's own register. **The batch's only two bold uses are now italics**, which is the house emphasis. Ilsa Kern's boot line is on the **twenty-second** of last month, the day the bank was cut, and she has been on the bank **since the twelfth** rather than for three days. The duplicated *the only one of the five that is not a plan* is now *the only one of the five that is a record of a thing done*. Anneke Roos's refusal to initial is made **at six in the morning**, the hour the note was written. The reed gauge is **twenty days** old, not twenty-one, in both places. Sera Quill's three silences are no longer dated to scenes she is not in, and one refusal is no longer counted as two: **nobody asked her a fourth time becomes nobody asked her again.** The *which is his habit / which is what he does* frame appeared six times in nine chapters and now appears three. The use log read aloud is **nine minutes** at the bench and nine minutes in the room, and the instrument's reading is about eleven seconds, which is a different thing.

### The quotation-mark defect, and its closure

**This is closed. Zero instances remain in the volume.**

The defect was systematic and mechanical: in many chapters a speech that runs over two or more paragraphs opens a quotation mark at the start of the first paragraph and does not close it at the end of that paragraph, and the next paragraph opens another. Chapters 1–11 never had it. From Chapter 12 onward the close prompt's census put it at 217; the detector found **219 at paragraph starts, plus two mid-paragraph instances in Chapters 20 and 37 that a paragraph-start detector cannot see, so 221 in all.** The per-chapter counts differed from the prompt's census by 26 and 35 in Chapters 26 and 35. **221 marks were added across 28 chapters: 12 (4), 17 (2), 19 (1), 20 (3), 21 (10), 24 (7), 25 (7), 26 (4), 27 (1), 28 (7), 29 (8), 30 (27), 31 (4), 32 (6), 33 (9), 34 (8), 35 (12), 36 (14), 37 (15), 38 (17), 39 (11), 40 (10), 41 (2), 42 (6), 43 (10), 47 (2), 48 (10), 49 (4).** Chapter totals before and after are in `reviews/volume-01.findings.md` § 13.

**Every chapter now balances. 5,106 marks, 2,553 pairs, no speech open at the end of a paragraph, no closing mark without an opening, and no odd chapter.** **The second audit found the two instances a per-chapter count cannot see** — a surplus pair in Ch 26 and a surplus pair in Ch 35 — and both are closed; see § *The second audit of the close*.

**Marks are straight everywhere, decided once, and written into `bible/terminology.md`.** This pass originally left Chapters 1–10 on curly quotation marks and apostrophes and Chapters 11–49 on straight ones, and declined to normalize on the grounds that a glyph is a typesetting decision and not a defect. **The second audit reversed that and the volume is now uniform: 1,886 curly glyphs in Chapters 1–10 were converted to straight marks and straight apostrophes, a glyph-for-glyph change with no word, number or date altered.** The nested quotation in Ch 10 is a straight single mark inside a straight double. **A later writer must not reintroduce curly marks, and must use the multi-paragraph speech rule: a speech that runs over paragraphs opens a mark in every paragraph and closes it in the last.**

## The Volume 01 close pass

Run against all forty-nine chapters after Batch 0005's hand review. **The close-up is `reviews/volume-01.findings.md`; this section is the record.** **Forty-one chapter files changed** — thirty for the repairs in §1–§5 and eleven more for the spelling pass in §6 alone — plus `bible/characters.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, the state records and the earlier review records. **A second audit of that close then touched twenty more chapters, listed in §8.** **No plot beat, no locked number, no name and no Movement 5 date was altered.**

### 1. Quotation marks — closed

221 closing marks across 28 chapters, and then two surplus pairs removed in Chapters 26 and 35 by the second audit. Full census and per-chapter counts are in § *The quotation-mark defect, and its closure*. **Zero instances remain; every chapter balances at 5,106 marks and 2,553 pairs, and every mark in the volume is straight.**

### 2. The day clock — twenty-four violations repaired

The rule is that a day in 1–30 is in October, the previous month, and is dated by its plain number or *of last month* or *of this season*, **never *of this month* and never as an ordinal of a month**; a day in 31+ is dated by its ordinal of the next month or *of this month* or its plain season day number, **never *of last month*.** Chapters 31–49 were clean. Chapters 1–30 carried twelve violations of the first kind and none of the second. **The first pass found and fixed these twelve, and then a second audit found twelve more that the first pass's detector had missed:**

| Chapter | Line | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 16 | 53 | the twenty-seventh of this month | the twenty-seventh |
| 16 | 135 | since the eleventh of this month | since the eleventh |
| 16 | 141 | on the twenty-seventh of this month | on the twenty-seventh |
| 16 | 145 | on the seventeenth of this month | on the seventeenth |
| 17 | 137 | on the fourteenth of this month | on the fourteenth |
| 18 | 65 | since the second of this month | since the second |
| 18 | 79 | on the third of the month | on the third |
| 19 | 115 | on the eighth of the month | on the eighth |
| 20 | 23 | on the third day of the month | on the third day |
| 20 | 51 | the well is worse than it was on the first of the month | the well is worse than it was on the first |
| 20 | 65 | on the twenty-seventh of this month | on the twenty-seventh |
| 21 | 27 | the twenty-first of the month (field log line) | the twenty-first |
| 23 | 51 | twenty-third of the month (a written entry) | twenty-third |
| 23 | 63 | on the twenty-third of the month | on the twenty-third |
| 23 | 89 | Monday, twenty-third of the month (the mill board) | Monday, twenty-third |
| 25 | 5 | on the twenty-fifth of the month | on the twenty-fifth |
| 25 | 63 | the twenty-fourth day of the month (sub-office record) | the twenty-fourth day |
| 26 | 51 | On the sixteenth day of the month | On the sixteenth day |
| 26 | 137 | twenty-sixth of the month (the field record) | twenty-sixth |
| 27 | 75 | on the morning of the twenty-seventh of the month | on the morning of the twenty-seventh |
| 28 | 35 | between the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth day of the month | between the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth day |
| 28 | 83 | on the twenty-seventh of this month | on the twenty-seventh |
| 30 | 11 | Twenty-fourth of the month, tenth hour and forty minutes | Twenty-fourth, tenth hour and forty minutes |
| 30 | 19 | And the twenty-fourth of the month is not | And the twenty-fourth is not |

**Four forward references to day 31 in Chapters 20, 21 and 25** — the first monthly report that is due on day 31 — **were normalized from *the first of the month* to *the first of next month*,** because a day 31 spoken about on days 20, 21 and 25 has not arrived, and *of next month* is the form the volume already uses for exactly that case (Chs 33, 40, 42, 43, 47, 48, 49). **A day 31 that is current or past keeps *of this month* or *the first of the month* (Chs 31 to 49), and that distinction is now explicit.** **The generic idioms — *the rest of the month*, *the actual shape of the month*, *the first of every month* — are not dates and were left alone.** The 86 references of the form *the Nth of the month* and *of this month* in Chapters 31–49 all mean day 31 or later, which is the ordinal of the new month and is permitted, and so are the ten references to *the first of next month* and *the first of the coming month*, which are day 31 spoken of before it arrives.

**The date form used in those chapters is the volume's own: a bare ordinal, the way Ch 16's margin note opens *Seventeenth.* and Ch 28's notes open *Twenty-second day, seventh hour.*** "Of this season" is used ten times in the volume and would also have been correct.

### 3. Two date slips

- **Ch 47.** Anneke Roos entered *the letter was written on the twentieth day of last month*. **Ch 18 has Marek write it at four o'clock on the nineteenth, Nia strap it that evening, and it goes out on the cart on the twentieth in Ch 20.** The entry now reads *written on the nineteenth day of last month and it went out with the consignment on the twentieth.* **This repair was recorded by the first pass and not made; the second pass made it.** **The letter was returned sealed and unopened on the nineteenth and that is unchanged**, and so is the fact that nobody in the fen writes down why it was not delivered. **The underlying sequence was checked and holds:** Ch 18 is day 19 and the carter Nia names for seven the next morning is the morning of day 20, which is the morning Ch 20 opens.
- **Ch 49.** The new run of thirty-one yards was dated as licensed *on the twenty-first day of last month*. **The license note in Ch 28 is the twenty-second, fourth hour, licensed, S. Quill — the same hour the pipe was laid in Ch 22.** Now the twenty-second.

### 4. The Ch 29 / Ch 30 overlap — closed

The defect was that Ch 29 dated the three-acre crust to a Wednesday and the banks to a Friday, while Ch 30, 31, 40, 43 and 47 all say the crust came up on the Sunday. **Ch 29 now has the crust come up under his boot on the Sunday morning in one sheet about an inch and a half thick, standing on the edge of the hole he made in it, unchanged through Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday; and the banks beginning to come away at the top on the Monday and rolling when he put his boot into one on the Friday.** Ch 30's closing field walk was rewritten in its own words and re-dated to that Sunday crust, which also removed six paragraphs it had cloned from Ch 31.

**Ch 30's date, the fourth arm check, the eleven-hundred-word statement and the form's fourteen days did not move.** Ch 30's rewritten close also dropped an unsupported count and an unsupported "nine weeks", and now says the mill figure has moved *in the last eight days*, which is the twenty-second to the thirtieth.

### 5. Three genuine prose clones broken

- **Ch 19 / Ch 21**, the pressure pulse coming up through the boards — Ch 21 now has it come up *under* the boards.
- **Ch 16 / Ch 27**, Dunmar Fitch entering the page number and the hour — Ch 27 now has him rule the line first.
- **Ch 30 / Ch 31**, the parallel field walks — see above.

**What remains shared is house style and is deliberate:** *with the hour on it and read it back*, *the licensed person present column said none*, *lifted the sacking at the six o'clock corner*, *squared it to the edge of the table*, the two monthly examinations, the arm check, the miller's figures entered as a thing reported, and **the three things, which must stay word for word.**

### 6. Spelling — decided, US, applied

See § *Spelling, decided and recorded*. 111 instances across the forty-nine chapters, seven in the bible, and 85 in the state and review records. **Decided once. Not to be revisited.**

### 7. What the audit found good and did not touch

The weep chain (quarter of an inch twice measured through Chapters 19, 20, 31, 40 and 41, half an inch and a sixteenth by two rules at the seventh hour of the fourteenth in Ch 43, unchanged at the seventh hour of the fifteenth, and never mended); the compost arithmetic (400 hundredweight at 4s is £80, the 399 shortfall is £79 16, £4 5 is twenty-one hundredweight and a quarter); the mill figures (126, 131, 254, 231, 181, 419, with 261 the highest the board had and 419 higher than anything on it in thirty-one years); eighty a day for six years as 175,200; the return main at twenty-two rods of eight inch against forty-one of twelve in all six of its appearances; the £60 10, the fourteen days, the £14 10, the 399 hundredweight, the three and a half degrees, the hundred and thirty head, the forty-one houses, the two hundred yards, the four days, the six section-nine notes, the four records on the bench and the three stamped faces; the month-name rule in all forty-nine chapters; and every standing guardrail, none of which the volume broke.

**One prompt figure is not a name in the prose.** There is no set called *the four rules of the return*. The four rules governing a return-line entry are in **Ch 48's fifth sheet and are consistent with Ch 31's first return line and Ch 47's entry: same book, same page, same hand, with the hour.** Ch 48's return clause separately carries three requirements. Recorded rather than invented.

### 8. The second audit of the close, and the fifteen chapters it touched

**This is a review of the close pass above, not a second close.** The review phase read `reviews/volume-01.findings.md` against the prose and returned six findings. **All six are applied, and three more were found while checking the six.** **No chapter was restarted, no plot beat was moved, no number was changed, and no date inside Movement 5 was touched.** **Twenty chapter files changed: 1 to 10 for the mark glyphs, the compost figure and the register of the weir girl; 20, 21, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28 and 30 for the day clock and the day-31 form; 26 and 35 for the surplus quotation pairs; 47 for the date slip; 37, 38, 41, 44 and 47 for the five-word phrase; and 9, 10, 16 and 26 for the same girl.**

1. **Two surplus quotation pairs, the kind a per-chapter count cannot see.** A closing mark with nothing open pairs up as an empty quotation, so the chapter total stays even and the paragraph looks closed. **Ch 26 line 89 was one speech and had a second closing mark at its end; the surplus mark is removed. Ch 35 line 21 had lost the opening mark of the form's own wording, which runs from *the root line beneath the windbreak* to *a damage to the node*; the opening mark is restored and the closing mark stays, so the third quotation in that paragraph now has both.** Volume total: **5,106 marks, 2,553 pairs.**
2. **The day-clock repair was half done while every state file said it was finished.** Twelve further instances of the same class survived, four of them inside documents. **All twelve corrected** — Ch 21 line 27, Ch 23 lines 51, 63 and 89, Ch 25 lines 5 and 63, Ch 26 lines 51 and 137, Ch 27 line 75, Ch 28 line 35, Ch 30 lines 11 and 19 — **and four day-31 forward references in Ch 20, 21 and 25 normalized to *of this month*.** The full table is in §2 above and the total is now **twenty-four**.
3. **The Ch 47 date slip was recorded as fixed and was not fixed.** The entry said *the twentieth day of last month*. **It now says *written on the nineteenth day of last month and it went out with the consignment on the twentieth*,** which is Ch 18 and Ch 20. **The Ch 49 half of that slip had landed and is untouched.**
4. **A required section of the close-up was missing.** The close prompt asks for the guardrails item by item *and* the relationship and power progressions item by item; the first pass wrote only the guardrails. **§ 11 and § 12 of `reviews/volume-01.findings.md` are new and are drawn from the prose and from this file.**
5. **Quotation-mark style was undecided in a pass that decided spelling.** **Straight marks govern, decided once, written into `bible/terminology.md`, and applied to Chapters 1–10** as a glyph-only pass over 1,886 curly quotation marks and apostrophes. The diff is glyph-for-glyph: no word, no number and no date changed.
6. **The figures did not reconcile.** The volume was reported as 165,844 words and measures **166,022** by `wc -w` over the forty-nine chapter files including their headings, 165,595 excluding the heading lines. **The method is now stated wherever the figure appears.** The spelling pass converted 111 instances and `state/current.md` said 110; **current.md now says 111** and both files carry the note that a whole-word count reads nearer 104 for the same pass.


7. **Found while checking the six: two clauses of the close-up's own volume-shape table were in the wrong movement, and one of its claims was not true.** `reviews/volume-01.findings.md` § 2 placed the surrender of the clean record in Movement 2; **it is Chapter 38, day 38, at two o'clock, on a form, and Lissa Vale says she is not sorry.** The same table said the instrument is seized; **it never is** — the application is made in Ch 37, won on the *so far as it goes* lock in Ch 38, produced on a form, and in Ch 48 the officer rules that nobody in the room may open it. **The table is corrected in place and now carries a note saying what was wrong.** And the claim that no sentence appears three or more times was false: *"Quarter of an inch," she said, from below* appears at Chs 21, 31 and 41, which is the volume's refrain at the monthly examinations and is deliberate. **The state files have said all three of these things correctly throughout; the error was in the close-up alone.**
8. **Found while checking the compost arithmetic: *two hundredweight* is ambiguous and was made *two hundred hundredweight* in six places** — four in Ch 6, one in Ch 18, one in Ch 21. The figure is 200 hundredweight everywhere, since 200 × 4s is the £40 that is three fees and Ch 43 puts the same rate on £14 10 as seventy-two hundredweight and a half. **The volume's own style is not ambiguous** — it already says *four hundred hundredweight* fourteen times — so this is the same sentence with the number that carries it spelled out. **No figure changed.**
9. **Found while checking the six, and the one that mattered most for the handoff: the Fieldbook panel count was wrong by half.** **There are four panels in the volume — Ch 6, Ch 19, Ch 24 and Ch 46 — one in each of Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0005, and none in Batch 0004**, each batch prompt allowing exactly one. The state files had said two since Batch 0002 because the Batch 0001 stage accounting never recorded the Ch 6 panel; that accounting, the close-pass line and § 12 of the close-up are now corrected together. **The same sweep corrected four more claims:** *so far as it goes* is **five** words and five chapters called it six (Chs 37, 38, 41, 44, 47) while Ch 38 itself already said five; the four day-31 references in Chs 20, 21 and 25 read *the first of next month* because that day has not arrived when they are spoken; **the weir girl of twenty-three is a woman in Ch 47 and a girl in Chs 9, 10, 16 and 26, and is a woman in all of them now**; and the close-up's *four holders in six years* contradicted Ch 46's *four times that I know of* over nineteen years. **No figure, date or plot beat moved in any of them.**

**What the second audit confirmed and did not touch:** the quotation balance, the spelling, the duplication scan, the Ch 29 / Ch 30 overlap, the weekday chain, every guardrail, the six elements of the final image, the forbidden terms, and the scope discipline — no Volume 02 prompt was created, no chapter was added, and there is still exactly one next phase.
