# VOLUME 02 — BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 50–59, MOVEMENT 1

**Volume 02, *The Graft Market*. Nine chapters' worth of apparatus and ten chapters of prose. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-02.md` and the batch's cards are `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`; both were written in the same phase as the prose and neither is drafted canon. Where a card and a chapter disagree, the chapter governs and this file is corrected. Volume 01 is closed and is not reopened here.**

## Volume 02 dating rule, which adds one thing to Volume 01's

**The governing table is under *Batch 0003 day clock* and *Batch 0005 day clock* and both still stand. Volume 02 adds one situation Volume 01 never had, and this is the whole of the addition:**

**By day 61, three months are in play at once.** October (season days 1–30), the month after October (days 31–60), and the month after that (day 61 onward). From day 61 onward:

| Which day | How the prose may date it |
|---|---|
| An October day (1–30) | **the bare number only** — *the fourth*, *the eighteenth*, *the twenty-eighth*. **Never *of last month* and never *of this month*, because October is now two or three months back and a reader will not be able to place it.** |
| A day in 31–60 | **the bare number**, or *of last month*, or *the twenty-first of a month*. Never *of this month* — that now means the new month. |
| A day in 61+ | **the ordinal of the new month** (*the first*, the second, the fourth), or *of this month*, or a bare number where the month is already fixed in the scene. |
| A day 31 or later spoken of **before** it arrives | **the first of next month**, or *the first of the coming month*. |

**What Volume 02 Batch 0001 actually used, and it held without a violation in ten chapters:** the bare ordinal for every October day, *of last month* only for the month after October, *of this month* only for day 61 onward, and *the first of next month* three times in Ch 51 and Ch 58 for a day that had not yet arrived. **October is named nowhere in Chapters 50–59**, and it is the previous month twice over; if a later batch wants to name it, the only safe use is the fixed historical one, the flock going up the fen road on the tenth of October, and never on a document.

**No document in Chapters 50–59 carries a month name, and one of them cannot: the district's own register of authorisations carries a day and a month as two bare numbers under a printed head with no word on it, and that is stated on the page and is how the district has kept the table for eleven years without a month ever having to be true of anything.** That is the batch's one dated document and it is dated in the volume's own convention, not the Crown's. **Corrected on review: the first draft of Chapter 59 had the reader name *September* and *November* aloud while reading that page, which destroyed the argument Chapter 53 spends its hinge on. The rule as it now stands and as a later batch must keep it: *a character may name a month in speech, but nobody in Tarrow Fen may name the month on a district form, because the form does not carry one, and the scene in Chapter 59 turns on that.* The register's order date is spoken as **one and nine**; the day on the November order paper is spoken as **a day in the eleventh month**. **October is not named anywhere in Chapters 50–59**; Chapter 58's willow ditch is dated *on a Sunday two months back*, which is October in the volume's own idiom.

## Volume 02 Batch 0001 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Ch 50 day 53 (**Wed, the twenty-third**) · Ch 51 day 54 (Thu, the twenty-fourth) · Ch 52 day 55 (**Fri, the twenty-fifth**) · Ch 53 day 56 (Sat, the twenty-sixth) · Ch 54 day 57 (**Sun, the twenty-seventh**) · Ch 55 day 58 (**Mon, the twenty-eighth**) · Ch 56 day 59 (Tue, the twenty-ninth) · Ch 57 day 60 (Wed, **the thirtieth**) · Ch 58 day 61 (**Thu, the first of the month after that**) · Ch 59 days 62–64 (Fri the second, Sat the third, Sun the fourth).

Day 52 was a Tuesday, locked in Volume 01 Chapter 49. **The only weekday statements in this batch are these ten**, and no chapter may say a weekday that is not in this table. **Chapter 55 has Vesta Lund arriving on the twenty-eighth, a Monday, and Chapter 51 has the Crown seed-route map arriving on the twenty-fourth, a Thursday, and the district's C/4 arriving on the Sunday post on the twenty-seventh, a Sunday** — the weekly post is on the Sunday, which is a new local fact and is load-bearing for Chapter 54's arithmetic.

## The day-to-chapter map as used

The cards supplied a map and it was followed with **four deliberate departures, all recorded here rather than made silently.** The cards put the *Crown-held column* discovery in Ch 56; it landed in Ch 53, because the extract has to be in the reader's hands for two days before the map has anything to be laid over. The cards put the *provenance bead* and the rootmark in Ch 57 with one demonstration; it is **two** demonstrations, because Tova Reed's demand to see it work on a line she knows is wrong is the beat that turns the volume and it cannot share a paragraph with the demonstration that works. And the cards gave Ch 59 as day 62; it runs days 62 to 64, because the reader's identification of a hand and the seed house's last eleven days cannot both happen in an afternoon. **And the cards gave the reader's last act as four lines out of the March's own book; the chapter gives her three, and the difference is recorded rather than smoothed: the card's four belongs to the March's second indexing, which is a different document from the seed house's own variety ledger, and a reader of hands who had never seen the March's book cannot name four lines she has never read.**

| Ch | Days | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 50 | 53 | the twenty-third. The charter's second clause starts working on the cooperative. Anneke Roos's sentence. The three acres. **The seventh section-nine note.** The demand and the rider |
| 51 | 54 | the twenty-fourth. **Nobody moves** on the levy. The sixteen against the forty-one. Tova refuses the map and says why. The leak, measured |
| 52 | 55 | the twenty-fifth. **Ruben Ashe and sixty pounds.** Four refusals for four correct reasons. The broker reads the books |
| 53 | 56 | the twenty-sixth. **The extract and the register page.** Perrin Osse's note. **The *Crown-held* column.** The dash row and the hold that is a decision |
| 54 | 57 | the twenty-seventh, Sunday. Gael Senn finds the clerk's hand. The leak entry. **The C/4, addressed to Cray** |
| 55 | 58 | the twenty-eighth. **Vesta Lund and a shilling a line.** The March indexed twice. **The map, form S/4** |
| 56 | 59 | the twenty-ninth. **Two maps on one table.** The legend. Nobody writes *concealment*. The eighth mark |
| 57 | 60 | the thirtieth. **Coll Renner, the method, and the provenance bead.** Tova's demand. **The panel.** **The graft and the rootmark** |
| 58 | 61 | the first of the month. **The first examination under the charter.** The ninth beat. The load test that was not performed. **The term closes with the closing of the sowing** |
| 59 | 62–64 | the second to the fourth. **Osvalde Bream, a day's cart, and a hand.** Two dates twenty-seven months apart. Lissa Vale's refusal. Three names in a ledger |

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 50–59

A later writer must not change these without recording the change. Every figure below is additive except where a Volume 01 lock is named, and nothing in this batch changed a figure established earlier.

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| **The charter's term** | **closes with the closing of the sowing; the closing of the sowing in this district is the last day of the month after next; the charter therefore expires on the twenty-ninth of that month, which is fifty-nine days from day 61, and there is no appeal anywhere in the four clauses.** Clause one says *for the season* and **no other date is on the instrument** — the term is fixed by the season-end form, at its foot, in the same hand as the box, as though it were a note | Ch 58 |
| **The season-end form** | **water-supply bylaw, sixth part. Schedule of maintenance, conditional charter, season-end. To the holder, to the examiner, and to the district.** **One box, and above it two words: *is maintained*. No third answer.** Consequential sentence: where a charter is conditional and provision is made for examination at the term and the examiner does not return this part, the charter lapses at the end of the term without notice, and the holder may apply in the ordinary way, and the ordinary way is a matter for the district and not for the holder. **Answered by the examiner on the first of a month, in her own hand, at about the second hour of the afternoon** | Ch 58 |
| **The district's records demand** | **water-supply bylaw, second part, form C/4. Records held by a party. To the holder of a charter, or to a party to the seed arrangements of a chartered node, or to both.** Served **on the Salt March Dryfoot Trust, of Cray**, on the twenty-seventh, by the Sunday post, **within fourteen days**, requiring any record touching the node, its head, its branches, the water it has drawn, the seed held or grown upon it, or the ground supplied by it, and a copy for the district file, **and offering a copy of anything produced to any other party to the annex** | Ch 54 |
| **Cray** | **nine hundred miles by the March road and a hundred and eleven miles further by the ordinary cart road; the March road is not a road, it is a line of about forty farms with a good surface on eleven miles of it.** Out two days, back two days, fourteen allowed: **ten days of slack on paper and nine of them in practice** — corrected on review; the first draft said eight, and fourteen less four is ten, and a cart that had gone down on the Thursday would have been back at the Fennmere counter on the fifth. The Thursday post is not optional and has not been since before Nia Vale left on a hired cart | Ch 54 |
| **The chapter's assistance clause** | the charter permits the holder to require the assistance of a person in the district water record, **at the holder's cost, and the holder may not name the person.** This is what puts Osvalde Bream on the road | Ch 59 |
| **The works extract, as produced** | **six columns, ninety rows, the district's own certified copy, unrecallable, the second issue of this season and dated, and NOT ONE HEAD PRINTED OVER ANY OF THE SIX COLUMNS — which is Volume 01 Chapter 16's reading of the same sheet and is still correct.** The columns are: item, order date, execution, execution, **held**, execution. The caption is **not a row of column heads**: it is the line of type at the top of the sheet, in the same type and the same line as the form number and the place for a date, and it lists the columns in their order, **and its sixth word is *Crown-held*.** One row is a school, with a hold in the fifth column. **The sixth column holds a letter in every row: Y in forty-one rows, N in thirty-one, a dash in the other eighteen**, and this yard had been calling it a third execution since the spring. N-2214/K is **Y**, and has been Y since the first of a month **in the year before he went in**, and **there is no column anywhere on the sheet that says what Y does**. **Nothing was hidden: a dash in a column of figures looks exactly like a date that has not happened yet, and the caption has been in the same type as the entries on every copy the district has ever sent out** | Ch 53 |
| **The hold, and what a hold is** | **the hold is the fifth column and it is printed in the same hand and on the same line as a date, because a form is a form and the printer is not thinking about it; the cell carries a word, then a date or a hand, then an hour, and a *by and at*.** **Thirty-one rows carry a hold. Eleven of the thirty-one also carry a dash in the sixth column; nine of the eleven have a date under the hold; two do not, and both of the two say *suspended* in print and neither has been released.** In the other twenty rows with a hold there is a date under the hold and a hand under the date. **A hold is not a job that has not started. A hold is a job that somebody has decided not to finish.** **The district's own printed word for a Crown hold is *suspended*** | Ch 53 |
| **The dash row, and it is a dash and never was a C** | row thirty-one: a colony cut out of a bigger holding eleven years ago, on the sheet from the year it was made, and in **the table's fourth year, eight years ago**, the bigger holding failed and the district put what was left into the Crown's hand. **Its hold is *suspended* with a hand under it and NO date and no release, and the same word is printed in the first issue of this season and the second, eight years apart, with a gap in the middle.** There is no letter anywhere in that row. There is a **dash** in the sixth column, and there are eleven of those dashes on rows that carry a hold | Ch 53 |
| **The register page, as produced** | **one certified page of a district register of authorisations, for item N-2214/K, certified as a true copy of the page and not of the log, and the log is not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it.** A printed head with a form number in the corner, a rule, **eleven rows and EIGHT COLUMNS, with a head printed over the first six and nothing whatever over the seventh or the eighth — no rule, no word, not a hairline.** Item N-2214/K. Order date, **the first of a month, in the year before he went in.** Executed, **10, 24, 27, —.** Held: released, date, hour, by and at *schedule clerk, fen drains.* **Then two unheaded columns, each filled in a hand. The seventh is *authorised by* and the eighth is *work description*, and BOTH OF THOSE NAMES ARE THIS BUILDING'S AND NOT THE DISTRICT'S, and they are entered as ours and not as the district's.** No instruction on the sheet saying what a hand in either is, and no legend under either. **No column for what the cut was for, because a register is not a record of a reason, it is a record of an instruction.** **The authorising column is therefore the SEVENTH, and a later chapter that says sixth is wrong** | Ch 53 |
| **The three executions, against Volume 01** | **10, 24 and 27.** Two are the district's. **The twenty-fourth was cut on this holding with a pair of shears.** The last of the three is the cut face a licensed reader put her hand on in the mud on the seventeenth. **This agrees with `New locks from the drafted Batch 0005` and the `State after Batch 0005` list of three stamped faces and does not contradict either** | Ch 53 |
| **The hold released on a Monday** | **nine days before he went into the shaft.** The shaft is eight years back, in the spring; the order date is seven months before that, so **the order date falls in the year before the year of the shaft and the order date is the first of a month, spoken as one and nine, and no chapter may put the order in the same calendar year as the shaft** | Ch 53, Ch 59 |
| **The two dates** | the register's order date is **the first of a month, in the year before the year of the disappearance, seven months before it, and is carried on the page as the two bare numbers one and nine.** The fieldbook's figure is **a year and eight months after it.** **The two are twenty-seven months apart and both are in the same hand.** *Probably a copying error, to be treated as one* still stands in Marek's own finding and has not been tested | Ch 59 |
| **The authorising hands** | **in nine entries out of ten the hand is even, in the same angle, in the same inclination, on a printed table filled by a clerk: a clerk's hand is even because the next clerk has to read it.** The **fifth** entry in the sixth column is not even: **the second half of the word *schedule* leans about four degrees to the left of the first half, and the tail of the *c* is doubled back as though the nib had been dragged.** That is a man writing fast, or in a hurry, or writing a thing he is putting into a book he does not want to have written. **Osvalde Bream will not say which, and says it is not in the wood and not on the page and not going to be in anything the district will produce in a year** | Ch 53, Ch 54, Ch 59 |
| **The identification** | **it is Aldren Vale's hand**, on a sheet of order paper for **a hundred and forty rods of twelve inch**, on **a day in the eleventh month of a year** — the month is a number and not a name, because a district form does not carry one — written at about the speed of a man writing while thinking about a wire, **and he made the W in *wire* the way he made the W in *weir*, and she has never in thirty-one years seen another hand make a W that way.** Put to her name | Ch 59 |
| **The Crown seed-route map** | **form S/4. Compiled for the use of seed holders and traders. Not a survey. Four cultivation districts, a district estimate against each, the district's recognised holding boundaries, the nine-mile road, the mill, the sub-office, and every watercourse anybody has paid the district to know about. Nine years old. About two feet by three, printed in five colors, a scale in one corner, and a legend in a box at the bottom with nine marks in it and a sentence under the box.** A hundred and forty going out this winter | Ch 55, Ch 56 |
| **The legend's ninth mark** | **unmapped root line, maintained in schedule.** Used **four times** on the sheet. **One of the four is the Weir Row branch.** The branch is not on the sheet as a watercourse; there is a printed line in the same weight as the other lines and it is a boundary, and the holding is Crown-held. **Marek's margin, on the face of the sheet, in his own hand: the mark is not a warning to the holder, it is a handover to whoever is not the holder** | Ch 56 |
| **The low quarter's own survey** | about three feet by two, on a single sheet of lining paper, made by four men with a stick, a bucket of lime and a knotted cord, on the nineteenth and twentieth of a month, **at their own hand and at their own cost, for their own use**, sent up the fen road on request and **uncharged**, with a line in a hand that is not a clerk's: *by four of us, at our own cost, for our own use, and anybody who wants it to be a survey can go and pay somebody.* **The only survey of that ground anybody has ever had** | Ch 56 |
| **Ruben Ashe's offer** | **sixty pounds for the charter. Forty pounds a year for the compost for as long as he holds it, quarterly, in advance, without a receipt. And the schedule of damage.** **In nine years he has bought thirty-one conditional charters, held each through its term, and sold the conditions; eleven holdings have gone to nobody in that time and he bought four of them and did not save any of them and does not say he did.** The price is the same sixty pounds the district entered for water four wells in Fennmere did not get, and **neither number is worth anything to him, and he says so, and he says the water stopping is not on him, it is on the end of the season, and the end of the season was in the document before the body signed it.** **He comes back at the end of the season**, and Dunmar Fitch enters that on his own initiative | Ch 52 |
| **Vesta Lund's offer** | **a shilling a line for every line of the seed house's ledger that names a variety, and the March's cart will carry the fen's material north at her cost.** **She does not want the seed and says so in the first minute.** Forty-four, of the Ashcombe index, twenty-two years, printed every two years, **nine hundred smallholders who cannot afford to find out anything else.** She has been reading maps like S/4 since she was twenty-one | Ch 55 |
| **The March indexed twice** | **once eleven years ago, in Tova Reed's first year as steward, when she was twenty and had been a steward four months** — a gentleman from the Crown's own seed office in Auberron, three weeks on circuit with a clerk and a list, took the trust's lines out one at a time into a printed table, put no trust mark on any of them, and said the words *long-term preservation*. **What the March got was a set of glass plates and a shelf in a dry room in Cray and it is still there.** He copied the Ashcombe black oat into the wrong column and printed four hundred copies, **so the most widely believed name in this district is a man in Auberron's handwriting, not hers.** **Once six years ago** — a list of four named lines in an appendix to somebody else's book, no mark on them, in a printer's file in four counties, source given as the Dryfoot Trust, Cray, **and four names struck out of the March's own book in a hand that is not any of theirs, in a color that is not theirs, dated, and a removal is a fact about a book.** The March got a guarantee and the guarantee was that no copy of its varieties would leave the trust without the trust's mark on it | Ch 55 |
| **The two categories the index does not print** | **a line held by one person in one garden with no name anybody would print, because a printed name is a claim and a claim can be sued on; and a line held by nobody anybody knows of and is grown, because printing that tells nine hundred people it can be had.** Both carried by Lund for twenty-two years. **She says the woman who has been indexed twice must not tell her she approves, because she has had that from a Crown seed office** | Ch 55 |
| **Coll Renner's offer** | **two hundred a year, the day work, a yard, a copper clip, and the method in writing under his name and his license** — which means the liability is his and not Marek's, and **he does not want Marek's name on it.** Nine men at Fennmere, four of whom would do a holding for a season. **What he wants is the order of the work, not the readings** | Ch 57 |
| **The provenance bead** | a copper collar, turned, with a bead of living rootwood the size of a hazel nut, in a tin bed of damp sand. Cut out of a grafted seed. **Laid against a wetted seed lot, the lot reports where it was grown, in four seconds, without a ledger and without a clerk.** It worked four times out of four and did not vary. **A graft keeps the thing you put in and loses the thing that was there, and the losing is not a fault, it is what the word means** | Ch 57 |
| **The bead's demonstration on a wrong line** | **a black oat out of the Ashcombe**, in the March's book eleven years, arrived on a cart in a tin with no label through a man who wanted to be helpful, **not the Ashcombe oat and not anything anybody can name.** The bead reported **Ashcombe, and the season before last, and the ground was under a ley.** **It is wrong, and it is a better answer than eleven years of arguing, and it is a lie, and it is not a lie anybody told** | Ch 57 |
| **The batch's one Fieldbook panel, spent in Ch 57** | **read by Coll Renner, licensed, at the copper seam, on a trestle in the yard, with the use-log line written and read back by Lissa Vale first and the contact tested with a plain wire gauge and written down before he went on.** Three plain lines, **the instrument's and not a person's:** *A bead will hold what is grafted into it. / It will not hold what was there first. / Put your name on which of the two you are keeping.* **No document in this batch quotes it as an instruction or as a reason, and the batch's last chapter now ESTABLISHES, on the page, that it is spent on a question that turns out not to be the question. This was a promise the first draft did not keep and the repair pass paid: on the third, Osvalde Bream looks at the rootmark and asks why nobody has laid the instrument near a page, and the answer is that the three lines are about a bead and what a bead holds and what a man is going to keep, that a bead goes against a wetted lot and cannot be laid against paper at all, that the question in the room for four days was a hand on a page, and that there is no instrument in this district that will read a page. Dunmar writes none of it down, and says why: a book that puts a bead's rules in beside a page about a hand has told a district clerk the wrong thing about both** | Ch 57, Ch 59 |
| **The first skin rootmark** | **the inside of the left forearm along the vein, four inches, branching twice, not straight, not following a vein, the color of a healed burn and not a bruise.** It came up between the sixth hour and the seventh hour on the thirtieth. **The graft was a flowerpot of the March's rye on a trestle, at twenty past five, with Renner's clip, Renner's license, Lissa Vale's use-log line, Gael Senn as witness, and Dunmar Fitch reading it back. Coll Renner wrote four lines and the hour in his own book and handed it to the clerk: a rootmark, a record of what he connected and not of what it was worth, permanent, could not be stopped and would not have been, and he was glad of it. Nobody in that yard wrote the word *became*** | Ch 57 |
| **The leak, on the twenty-seventh and on the first** | **five-eighths of an inch, twice measured, two rules, in daylight, at the seventh hour, pump on and continuous.** Half an inch and a sixteenth on the fourteenth, at the seventh hour, in daylight. **One sixteenth in thirteen days — corrected on review; the first draft said three-sixteenths in nine days, and both the fraction and the interval were wrong, and the day before the fourteenth there were nineteen hours of flood on the head of the same node, which is why the two ends of the interval are not the same kind of day.** Marek refuses to write a rate: **there are two readings thirteen days apart.** On the twenty-sixth the interval from the fourteenth is **twelve days**. Still a leak, still not mended, still cause not established, still into the housing and not into the pit, and the pit has four feet of iron water in it and is not up a quarter of an inch | Ch 51, Ch 54, Ch 58 |
| **The beat, ninth logging** | **eleven in the quarter hour, two runs of four and one of three, and the last three together, and the two fours about two minutes apart. Felt at the rail and not at the coping stone, which is the other way round from the twenty-first of a month.** Cause not established, licensed person present none, **and nothing written underneath it by the examiner's decision, with her name on the decision and not the thing's** | Ch 58 |
| **The third levy, and nobody moves** | sixteen households, **fourteen signatures, four pounds five**, and it is **the same sum and the same roster as ten days before.** Two put nothing in and put nothing in again; one put a shilling in and wrote *and I am sorry* under it again, in the same three words; two signed first, one of them the woman with the shilling. **Two of the sixteen did not sign and their names are not recorded, because the paper forbade it and Lissa Vale wrote the forbidding there herself. No motion was moved.** The paper came back with fourteen signatures and there is no explanation for the sum in the building, and **Marek enters that he looked and did not find one, and Dunmar Fitch insists it go in as an observation and not with a cause on it** | Ch 51 |
| **The levy against the bill** | **sixteen houses on the levy and forty-one houses on the bill**, and no form on which the two facts can be written together, and the four pounds five is being put into a wound nine miles from the people the bill is for. Entered in those words at Anneke Roos's asking | Ch 51 |
| **The compost, this month** | **nothing was put into the return main. None available to put in. Specification four hundred hundredweight a month; the three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight shortfall is carried forward and not discharged, and is seventy-nine pounds sixteen at four shillings.** The last thing put in was **twenty-one hundredweight and a quarter, on the fourteenth, by levy**, which is about a twentieth of a month's specification. The bed is at **three and a half degrees**. **The March's tin is forty-two days at risk on the first**, and forty-five on the fourth, and unmoved | Ch 51, Ch 58 |
| **The full-draw load test, not performed** | **for the first time in a season when it could have been, and Marek is the reason.** A charter to hang it on exists and did not on the twentieth. **A licensed engineer crossed the yard at about the eleventh hour on his own way to Fennmere and read the morning's number off the examiner's page without being asked and without touching anything, and will not be there tomorrow, and Marek did not send for anybody.** His reason, in his own words and in the return line: *he would rather have the figure and not have it; he wrote the book against himself so that it could be used, and he is not going to be the man who hands the district the one number that would help him, because he has learned that a number travels and does not come back.* **Gael Senn entered it in the second wording, in district charter law, in her own hand, under her own name, and if the district prefers a figure she is sorry and there is no figure** | Ch 58 |
| **The section-nine margin notes** | **eight, and the chain is now closed on the page: 6 at the end of Volume 01 → 7 in Ch 50 (the twenty-third) → still 7 in Ch 56 (the twenty-ninth) → 8 in Ch 58 (the first).** Volume 01 left six, of which **five are about a cut and the sixth, the compost note of Ch 49, is the only one that is not.** The seventh is Ch 50's, and it is **the second of the seven that is not about a cut** and the first that is a measurement, and the page says why the other five are cuts: a cut is a thing that can be refused, a measurement is a thing that can only be answered with money. The eighth is Ch 58's (four willow sets alive and the 399 shortfall) and **is the first of the eight that does not close with *this is worth nothing against a cut*, and the omission is on the page with Marek's name on it and his reason: *the eighth note is a record and not an argument.* **The closing sentence is SEVEN WORDS, not nine, and the legend on the Crown map is SIX WORDS, not four, and the note Tova Reed wrote on the face of that map, *no line here*, is THREE** | Ch 49 (Vol 01), Ch 50, Ch 56, Ch 58 |
| **The second form, refused on the Sunday** | **Marek wanted to write to Perrin Osse on Monday the twenty-eighth to ask whether another form would reach the register, and Dunmar Fitch refused at the table: the first form was answered in four days and the second one would produce nothing at all, on purpose, and a body that asks twice has told a district office that asking works.** No second form went. **A later chapter may not produce a second demand for the register, and may not say that one was sent** | Ch 54 |
| **The four lines Tova Reed has never indexed** | **four, and she has told nobody the number, including Vesta Lund, in Chapter 55.** A line is in an appendix in a printer's file in four counties, on a glass plate in a dry room in Cray not looked at in six years, and in one man's field, and when that man dies there is nothing left of it anybody can put a name to. **Tova has lost none in eleven years and holds four, and that is the standing reason she refuses every index.** A later batch may use the four and may not turn them into a rescue | Ch 55 |
| **Gael Senn's dates as examiner** | **named second on the certificate of the twentieth, which is ten days before anybody expected a certificate, and examiner of this node since the twenty-first of last month, which is the day the charter was made.** From the first of a month those are **the twentieth of last month** and **the twenty-first of last month**; no chapter may call either *of this month* | Ch 58 |
| **The cooperative's box** | **empty.** Counted twice. The first levy went to Sera Quill's fee; the second went to compost on the fourteenth; the third was not moved | Ch 51 |
| **The chapter's own note on consent** | clause two bars amendment of a condition this season without the consent of the body, **and a body cannot consent to the removal of its own record, because the removal is the consent.** Anneke Roos initialled the line; Dunmar Fitch entered it and said it is the most useful sentence anybody has put in front of him in nineteen years and that in six months one of the sixteen will want it out of the district's hands and he will be able to say this | Ch 50 |
| **The monthly root-change report** | **a charter condition, due on the first of a month, and nobody in the yard has written one since the twentieth of a month because the person who wrote it is on a hired cart and is not coming back.** Dunmar Fitch writes it. It is nine lines and it is the shortest one. Marek initials it. It goes to Fennmere and to Cray, **and nobody in the yard can say what the root changed, because the man who would have known cannot repeat a four-second contact** | Ch 58, Ch 59 |
| **Osvalde Bream** | **seventy-one. Thirty-one years of the crop histories of eleven holdings in three districts, out of a bookshop in a town with a market cross and a good bakehouse. Never licensed for anything and says so in the first minute.** Two rooms upstairs and she does not bring them and they are not in Cray and not in the district water record and not going to be. **She came two hundred miles for a day's cart and took no fee.** Her two conditions, both kept: **she reads hands, not names — a hand is not a man and a name is not a decision — and she will say who wrote it and will not say why and will not say whether anybody made him do it, and if a room asks her any of those three she will get up and go back down the road** | Ch 59 |
| **The three names she could not call wrong** | in the seed house's own variety ledger, three varieties: **a bean out of a place called the Ashcombe, an oat, and a thing they call here a Reed's Acre rye, which is not a rye and is not anybody's.** She has never seen any of them in any book anywhere and they are not in the printed index either. **She knows what their letters look like and cannot say that any of them is wrong, and says so: if anybody wanted to buy a line out of that book they could not tell.** She will not say whether the March's four struck lines are the same four. **Lissa Vale wrote the three names on a sheet, entered them against the ledger and not in it with the hour, and put the sheet on the middle of the table** | Ch 59 |
| **The two certification dates, which a reviewer will trip on** | **the standing pressure log was certified by Perrin Osse at the fourth hour of the afternoon of the seventh day of a month (Volume 01 Ch 37), and the clean record — the fieldbook, the last account of the instrument in the world — was lost at two o'clock on the eighth, when the hearing officer came to Tarrow (Volume 01 Ch 38).** Both dates are correct and they are different events. **Chapter 50 says the log cannot be added to since the seventh and that the clean record cost them the eighth, and Chapter 59 says the clean record cost the eighth of last month. Nothing in Volume 02 may move either date** | Ch 50, Ch 59 |
| **Lissa Vale's refusal, on the fourth of a month** | **she refuses, in advance, the question anybody in the yard wants to ask — whether that is his hand or a copy of his hand — and she refuses it before anybody has time to want it and after she has had about an hour to want it herself.** Her reason is about cost and is not about a book: *a name in a book costs this building; it cost the clean record on the eighth, it cost three acres on the sixteenth, and it cost a woman her day rate in a market she is not in.* She says nobody in the building will be asked, and that if a district officer asks in a year he will say the same thing and **she will have said it first and in her own name and not in his**, and that she does not know what the next thing will be and has decided to be the one who says so before the yard works it out. **It is not Sera Quill's silence. It is not Lissa's fourth refusal. It is not numbered, and nobody asked her about it, and it is not to be numbered later** | Ch 59 |
| **Tova Reed's map** | **in a locked case with the key on her belt, and the key was on her belt in Volume 01 too, and nobody in this yard has ever asked her what else is in it.** She is going to look at it every week of the season, and if the dry-land line ever comes back through a relay she is going to see whether the March is on it. **She will not say in front of a stranger whether she is keeping the map or giving it away** | Ch 51, Ch 59 |
| **Tova Reed's seventh line against the trust** | **on the face of a Crown-printed map, under the legend, in her own hand, in letters about the size of her own: *Nothing on this sheet about this ground. Nine years of it. And I have sold into it eleven years.*** And under that: *A map for buyers is not a survey and says so on its own face, and the district has printed the class of line it will not draw rather than leaving the paper white, and I want both of those in a book together, because in nine years one of us will need the second half and nobody will remember the first, and the half that makes us feel better is the half that is more dangerous.* The sixth was the annex line about the bed and the compost; **this is the seventh** | Ch 56 |

## New locks from the drafted Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **The charter's clock is dated and the date is the volume's spine.** **The term closes with the closing of the sowing; the closing of the sowing is the last day of the month after next; the charter expires on the twenty-ninth of that month.** Season day 120, which is the last day of Volume 02 Batch 0001's month and two batches short of the volume's own end. **A later chapter may move it only by recording the change, and a batch may not quietly turn the season into something else.**
- **A charter that has a term also has an answer to give.** The season-end form has one box and two words and no third answer, and **the second wording is now the only instrument this holding owns that can say *I looked and I could not find a number* in a room where somebody wants a number.** A later chapter may use it and may not extend it into a general right.
- **The works schedule is no longer structurally unavailable and must not become a plot device.** The refusal's ground — *a body holding no charter* — is gone, the extract is produced, and the reason is on the page and is a clerk's note saying nothing was decided. **The register behind the certified page is still not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it, and that must not be overturned without a reason the reader can follow.** The extract is the district's own copy and cannot be recalled.
- **The *Crown-held* column and the legend are Volume 02's structural answer and they are a category, not a conspiracy.** A line with no local holder is maintained by the district, on a schedule, and is drawn differently on a public map so that anybody can see that it is not the holder's. **Nobody in this batch wrote the word *concealment* or proposed it, and the proposal and the refusal are both on the page.** A later chapter may name the practice; **it may not turn it into a man in a room.**
- **A hold is a decision and not a beginning.** This is established on the page by a tenant farmer and it changes how every hold in the volume reads. **The printed word for a Crown hold is *suspended* and it is the same word in both issues eight years apart.**
- **The reader the charter names cannot be named by the holder**, and the cost is the holder's, and this is the mechanism by which the first professional opinion in the volume arrived and it is also the mechanism by which the cooperative made itself legible. **The assistance clause and the C/4 records demand are the same door and Volume 02 must not pretend otherwise.**
- **Nia is not in this batch and her absence is a working absence.** The monthly report is nine lines and short; the seed ledger is a seed steward's own book because a clerk does not have the names in his head; nobody in the yard can say what the root changed. **The letter is in the open drawer, sealed, and it is not opened and it is not discussed, and nobody may say she decided not to deliver it.**
- **The fieldbook is on the middle of the seed-house table and the use log is beside it, and anybody may ask to have either read.** It was opened twice in this batch's week — by Coll Renner, licensed, with the use-log line written and read back first, and only that. **The officer's ruling of the twenty-first of last month, that nobody in her room may open it and that the ruling binds her successor, is untouched and no officer has been in this yard since.**
- **The Fieldbook panel is spent and this batch's allowance is zero.** One panel per batch is available to Batches 0002 to 0005 and not before. **The Ch 57 panel is the instrument's and not a person's, is not a reason, and is not a document's authority for anything.** It is also, by the end of the batch, spent on a question the instrument cannot answer, **and that is now said out loud in Chapter 59 rather than left for a later writer to infer, so Batch 0002 does not inherit a debt that was never paid.**
- **Marek is Stage 2 with a rootmark and nothing else.** He can make a small reliable graft between living tissue and a limited local branch. He can keep two or three active links. **He still cannot draw, has no bead, no anchor work, no bridge above a local conduit, no seedheart, no caretaker link, and no reading of the deep archive, and the touchroot sense is unchanged and still unreliable.** The rootmark is a record of what he connected and **it is a record written in his own book by the licensed man who put it there**, and nobody in this volume may write the word *became*.
- **Nothing romantic happened and nothing may lean that way.** Tova Reed refused a broker and a buyer and a grafted seed in ten days, alone, in her own name each time, and Marek did not get to argue with one of them. **She is not a rescue and he is not saved.** The relationship milestone for this range is *shared records*: **they have one book, neither keeps a private copy, and the ledger is the same object in both their hands.** That is institutional and it is not tender.
- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and has not said anything about it.** Her refusal on the fourth of a month is **hers and is not Sera Quill's and is not her own fourth**, and it is about the cost of a name in a book and not about a book, and it is not numbered.
- **The batch's three buyers are all correct and none of them is a villain, and each of them is dated.** Ashe returns at the expiry, which is the charter's own date. Renner returns in a month with a form he described in advance. Lund's index prints again this winter and does not know this fen has a branch in it. **A later chapter may not convert any of the three into a threat, and may not soften any of them.**
- **The pathhound is not in this batch and nothing is written underneath it.** The collie's report is in three places, Gael Senn's decision to write nothing about the animal stands with her name on the decision, the low quarter's own map of the standing iron exists on paper in Fennmere, and **Marek still has not walked it and has not asked anybody to.**
- **The long line and the settlement with no seat are untouched and unnameable.** The word *seat* does not appear in Chapters 50–59, the settlement is not named, the terms of the refusal are not held by this body, and **no chapter quotes the long line, sizes it, places it, or enters it.** **Corrected on review: the first draft of Chapter 52 had the broker name it — *a long line two hundred yards under a windbreak belonging to ninety people with no charter* — and say it was in the annex. It was in none of the annex's four contents, sizing it to ninety people identified it, and the state file then recorded a false lock. The broker now says what the annex actually says, which is worse: the return main is half of specification by the length and a quarter by the bore, and the annex's fourth term states that the seed described on it may not come up, and that term is a line in a public ledger in Cray.** The burn lapsed on the twelfth of last month and the long line is intact.
- **Ivo Tarrant's four men do not come back.** The bypass is unbuilt, unfunded, and may not appear.
- **Dunmar's *none known* is spent. Marda Vole does not appear. The sealed envelope holding the first reader's record is never to be opened by anybody, ever. The Thornwild refusal's terms are not held by this body.**
- **The schedule of damage is not paid, not remitted and not reduced.** The broker's price is the same figure and neither number is worth anything to anybody, and that is the batch's joke and it is a real joke.
- **The third exchange requirement is still missing and the 399 hundredweight shortfall is carried forward and not discharged.** The graft is being fed about a twentieth of what it was designed to be fed, **and a licensed contractor has now said in a yard that eleven fen holdings in this district cannot afford a cooperative, which is the first time anybody has put the whole of this holding's position in a sentence that was not about this holding.**

## Stage and contact accounting after Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Marek is Stage 2, Grafthand, and the stage is one small graft old.** He can make a small reliable graft between a living plant and a limited local branch. He can keep two or three active links and a fourth fails. He cannot make a high-capacity bridge alone and he cannot safely attach himself to the network.
- **Contacts: four behind him, unchanged in kind and unchanged in cost.** The Volume 01 contacts stand — one supervised at the copper seam with Sera Quill on the eighth of a month, one deliberate and unsupervised with no bead and no return on the night of the tenth, and one on the scarroot of a cut face at Weir Row on the seventeenth. **The batch's graft is not a contact and is not counted as one; it is an operation, and it cost him a mark rather than a bleed.**
- **The first skin rootmark: present, four inches along the inside of the left forearm, branching twice, permanent, and recorded in this holding's book by Coll Renner under his own hand with the hour and the witnesses.** The volume's Stage 2 cost is paid on the page and the cost is a bill, not a badge, and Renner says so at the gate.
- **No draw, no return by rootlight, no anchor work, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive.** The market is a worse instrument than a draw and this volume uses it deliberately, and nobody in the fen has a way to refuse one.
- **The Fieldbook panel: one spent in this batch, in Chapter 57, by a licensed man, and the batch's allowance is now zero.** Four panels were spent in Volume 01 and none remains there. **Volume 02 has a fresh allowance of one panel per batch, and the next panel belongs to Batch 0002.**
- **Sera Quill has not been in the fen in this batch and has not been asked about anything.** She checked his arm four times in Volume 01 and not a fifth, and *the twice a week instruction* remains spent and may not be said again. **Her fourth refusal is unnumbered and unasked and may not be asked before Volume 03.**

## Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Volume 02 Batch 0001

Batch 0001 added nine things by drafting. All are local detail, mechanisms, forms, prices, places, or people, and none changes a world rule:

1. **Ruben Ashe**, a charter broker of Fennmere, who buys expiring conditional charters and sells the conditions on. His economics: thirty-one in nine years, eleven holdings gone to nobody, four of them his. **A price, a trade, and a class of man who exists because a charter expires.**
2. **Vesta Lund**, of the Ashcombe index, and **the index itself** — a printed list of landraces and their prices, bought by nine hundred smallholders, with two categories it does not print. **A market in seed history, and the two ways a printed name is dangerous.**
3. **The Salt March Dryfoot Trust's indexing history** — a Crown seed officer in Auberron eleven years ago and an appendix six years ago, and the four names struck out of the March's own book, and a black oat that is the most widely believed name in the district and is wrong. **A local history, and the volume's second illustration of a document that can be bought.**
4. **Coll Renner**, a licensed Rootwright contractor of the Tarrow district, and **the order of the work as a thing anybody can be asked for.** A profession, not a villain, and the first professional in the volume who is not a Crown officer.
5. **The provenance bead**, and with it **a graft as a market device** — a small living bead that makes a seed lot report where it was grown. **This is a new use of grafting and it is Volume 02's central mechanism, and the volume's rule about it is Renner's sentence: a graft keeps the thing put in and loses the thing that was there.**
6. **The records demand to a named party, form C/4, and the chapter's assistance clause.** Two mechanisms, one door.
7. **The season-end maintenance form** with one box, two words, a consequential sentence, and a term line at the foot. **A new form class, and the volume's clock.**
8. **The Crown seed-route map, form S/4**, with its nine-mark legend and the ninth mark. **A Crown map class, and the volume's answer to the volume's question.**
9. **Osvalde Bream**, a seed steward of seventy-one who reads hands and not names, and the Ashcombe, and the eleven holdings, and the bookshop. **A person, and a qualification the series had no use for until now.**

None of the nine changes a world rule, a power rule, a relationship lock, or the ending. **No new named member of the cooperative was created in this batch**, which continues the Volume 01 discipline: the crew is Ilsa Kern, Jonas Byre, Anneke Roos, Gael Senn, Lissa Vale and Dunmar Fitch, all of them already on the page, plus the two households of the levy who put nothing in and the man of about fifty and the man of about seventy, who are held by function and are not given names and may not be given names in a later batch without recording it.
